# Mendari vision audit: the repository against the launch plan

Audit date: 2026-10-05. Read-only. Branch `audit/vision-2026-10`.

## Audited commit

- **Commit audited:** `e55ed48c304ad26f110ad9f101be827358c674f0` ("Merge pull request #128 from Junebugg1214/claude/public-prep").
- **Why this commit:** it was `origin/main` when the audit started (`git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main`). The local branch named `main` is 77 commits behind it (`560d14ef`) and was not used.
- **Main moved during the audit.** By the end, `origin/main` was `fc0bec9f595e6ba68d499a42a6838c0067d3ff3d`: PR #130 (inbox automation gate) and PR #131 (`/docs` with sign-in on) merged, 62 files changed. This report still describes `e55ed48c`. One observation outside the audit, from reading two files at `fc0bec9f` and nothing more: `packs/patient-inbox-router/policy.yaml` lines 24 to 25 now carry `automation_requires: safety_screen: [rules_and_jev, rules_and_llm]`, the pack is version 0.4.0, and `tests/test_inbox_pack.py` gained 33 lines. So blocker 1 below may already be closed on `main`. It was not audited: whether the new test covers every configuration and the held-out set, and whether the pack's risk rationale and the Jev recipes were corrected, is unknown. LP-01 in Phase 4 is written to check that first.
- **The plan is older than the code.** The plan is dated 3 October 2026 and cites commit `0182417`. Twenty-one commits merged after that (PR #127 pack safety, PR #128 public prep; 86 files, +5,624/−503). Several of the plan's "today" statements are therefore out of date; they are listed in Phase 2.
- **Work in flight, not audited.** Other sessions were committing throughout. At the end of the audit, ahead of the audited commit: `claude/inbox-automation-gate` 5 commits (since merged as PR #130), `claude/community-packs` 8, `feat/dashboard-tables` 1, `claude/design-c` 3. None of it is in the audited commit. Where it bears on a finding, the finding says so.
- **Isolation.** All work ran in this session's own worktree. Nothing was written to the main working tree, another branch or shared state; no network call was made to a hosted service; no secret value was printed. `git status --short` was empty after every stage.

## How the audit was done

- The plan was split into 18 units: the vision principles, the licensing decision, the plan's "today" claims, the stage gates and owner checklist, prompts A1 to A13, and the checks the repository enforces today.
- Each unit was audited against the code by one auditor, then re-checked by a second, independent pass told to refute it. The second pass re-opened every item marked Done, Contradicts, blocker or high, re-ran the commands a finding rests on, and spot-checked the rest.
- A statement in a README, a doc, a note or `tasks/todo.md` was treated as a lead, never as evidence. Done means the code exists and a test or a command run during the audit shows it working.
- **Test suite, run once in the worktree:** `pytest -m 'not browser and not slow'` gave 1,848 passed, 273 skipped, 143 deselected, exit 0, in 201.93 s. Of the 273 skips, 270 are Postgres-only tests, which need a database the audit did not start, and 3 need a cached HL7 corpus; the 143 deselected are 142 browser tests and 1 slow test. So the audit confirms the default suite is green on SQLite only.

**Status words.** Done, Partial, Missing, Contradicts the plan (the code does what the plan forbids, or the opposite of what it requires), Unknown (the repository cannot show it; the item says what would settle it). **Basis:** verified (seen in code, a test or command output), inferred (reasoned from indirect evidence), unknown.

## Summary

Mendari is a strong engine with a thin trust layer. The decision engine, the pack format, the CLI, the audit chain and the standards work are real and tested. Almost everything the vision adds on top (hidden test sets, statistics, four-arm scoreboards, a registry that checks what it shows, a site network, safety notices) is missing or partial. No stage gate is met: of the five gates, none has a single prerequisite fully evidenced in the repository.

| Unit | Items | Done | Partial | Missing | Contradicts | Unknown | Blockers | High |

The same gap can appear under more than one unit (the missing synthetic-example label is counted under five), so the totals count plan elements, not distinct defects. Phase 2 opens with the blockers de-duplicated by stage.

### What is verified

1. **The inbox safety gap is real, and it is the default.** `packs/patient-inbox-router/policy.yaml` has no `automation_requires`. With the default offline providers the pack detects 4 of the 20 held-out emergencies and routes 7 of the 16 it misses to AUTO, as a routine nurse message. Reproduced by script, through the CLI, REST and CDS Hooks. No test covers it. A fix merged to `main` during the audit (PR #130) and was not audited.
2. **Secret and endpoint binding is already done.** PR #127 removed every pack field that named a secret or an endpoint, and a permanent regression test holds it. The plan still lists this as "Unsafe".
3. **Pack patterns still have no time limit.** A pack with `(a+)+$` validates and stalls a worker, and so does a small input schema with nested references and no pattern at all. Even the unmodified inbox pack takes 563 ms of CPU on one crafted 10,000-character message. There is no time budget on a decision.
4. **Nothing forces the hosted site to protect inputs.** The PHI mode defaults to `off`, all five packs store the full input, and nothing in the Vercel entrypoint requires another mode. With sign-in on and mode `off`, the server starts. There is no scheduled purge. A row written under `off` cannot be erased without breaking `audit verify`.
5. **No safety notice exists where a clinician looks.** The plan's notice text is nowhere in the tree. The dashboard says only "sandbox · synthetic data only". There is no acknowledgement before Try it, and "synthetic only" is not enforced on any entry point.
6. **No reference pack carries the synthetic-example label** on any of the four surfaces, and the pack format has no field that could hold it.
7. **There are no hidden sets and no statistics.** All 27 dataset files are tracked in git. No function in `src/` computes a confidence interval, a significance test or a resample. The shipped inbox card shows "20/20" in first place on 20 positives; the exact 95% interval for 20 of 20 is 0.83 to 1.00.
8. **There is no four-arm scoreboard.** The leaderboards hold 166 rows: 22 offline, 30 with Jev, 114 with an LLM, and none with both. 105 of the 166 rows were produced by an older version of the pack that carries them. The Decision Card ranks by accuracy alone.
9. **"Metrics only" is not true today.** `mendari eval --share` sends the dataset's record ids whenever a critical case is missed, and the registry stores them. The registry accepts unsigned site reports of any shape from any publisher token and shows them on the pack page.
10. **The registry shows what the uploader sent.** The Decision Card, benchmark and leaderboard on a pack page are the uploader's own files, word for word. A forged card was accepted and served. There is no yank, and a pulled pack can automate.
11. **Organizations exist; accounts do not.** Tenancy by identity-provider claim is built and decision data is isolated per organization. There are no users, memberships or invitations, and anyone whose token lacks the claim lands in the shared `default` organization.
12. **The repository's own plan runs ahead of the launch plan.** `tasks/todo.md`, dated one day after the plan, says to make the repository public now for community packs. That skips the plan's private stage and its gate. It also reuses the ids A1 to A11 and B1 to B10 with different meanings.

### What is inferred or unknown

- **The hosted site's settings are unknown.** PR #128 removed production values from the tree on purpose. Whether production runs with PHI mode `off`, unlimited daily caps or fork previews with secrets cannot be seen here. The Vercel environment for Production and Preview would settle it.
- **Every owner item in Part B is unknown** from the repository: secret rotation, NDA, patent and trademark steps, employer IP clearance, the legal owner, release-environment reviewers, branch protection. Each row says what record would settle it.
- **The plan's scores cannot be checked.** The launch-readiness report and the EHR scorecard are not in the repository at any commit, and neither is a "Use Cases page", which the shared header tells every prompt to update.
- **Whether CPT codes may ship under Apache-2.0 is a legal question.** The prior-authorization pack uses 15 AMA CPT codes by name. Counsel must decide; replacing them with a fictional code system removes the question.

## The top five blockers

| # | Blocker | Gate it blocks | Who | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The inbox pack automates missed emergencies in its default configuration, and no test would catch it (A4.1a, A4.1c). A fix merged during the audit and is unaudited | Stage 2 | Build | 1 to 3 days |
| 2 | Two plans disagree on when the repository goes public; following `tasks/todo.md` would publish with none of the eight stage-3 prerequisites evidenced (P7, TC10) | Stage 2 and 3 | Owner decision | Hours |
| 3 | The hosted site can store inputs in plaintext, keep them forever, and cannot erase what it already holds (A5.1a, A5.1b, A5.2a, A5.goal-legacy) | Stage 2, hosted access | Build and owner | 1.5 to 2.5 weeks |
| 4 | No research-preview or 911 notice, no synthetic-data acknowledgement, no synthetic-example label, and the secret scan the gate names has not been run as written (A4.2, A4.3a, A2.6a, A1.1b) | Stage 2 | Build | About 1 week |
| 5 | The trust layer does not exist yet: no hidden sets, no intervals or tests, no four-arm scoreboard, a registry that republishes the uploader's claims, and a share command that leaks case ids (A8, A9, A10.1a, A11.2b-ids) | Stage 3 and 4 | Build and owner | 8 to 12 weeks |

## Stage gates at this commit

| Gate | Prerequisites evidenced | What is missing first |
|---|---|---|
| 1 to 2, design partners | 0 of 6 | A1 step 1 as written (trufflehog, all refs, private report), A4, the synthetic-example label; owner: rotation, NDA, patent decision |
| 2 to 3, open-source launch | 0 of 8 | NOTICE, DCO text and check, A5, A6, A8 labelling workflow; owner: partners, trademark, terms and privacy notice |
| 3 to 4, scoreboards and pack store | 0 of 7 | Pattern time limits (A3), A7, A8 frozen sets, A9, A10, the grading method, a security review that covers the registry |
| 4 to 5, self-service | 0 of 6 | Kill switch, runbook, status page, hosted monitoring (A13); owner: firewall, SOC 2 |
| 5 to 6, clinical pilots | 0 of 6 | Site keys and a closed schema (A11), EHR token trust and mappings (A12); owner: clinician review, FDA opinion, site agreements |

"Evidenced" means fully shown by the repository. Owner prerequisites that leave no trace count as not evidenced, not as failed.

## Phase 1: Current state against the plan

One table per unit. Each row is one element of the plan with one status, a short statement of the current state and the first piece of evidence. Cells are shortened; "(+N)" counts the further evidence entries. The full text of every row, every evidence entry (file and line, test name, or command and output) and every recommendation is in [`vision-audit-evidence.md`](vision-audit-evidence.md), under the same IDs. Paths are relative to the repository root at the audited commit. The second pass made 358 corrections to the first-pass findings; the tables show the corrected result.

## Phase 2: Gap table

### Blockers, de-duplicated, by the stage they block

Each line groups the Phase 1 items that describe one gap. "Owner" means no prompt can do it.

**Before stage 2 (design partners self-host)**

1. Inbox pack automates missed emergencies by default; no test (A4.1a, A4.1c, A4.acc-1, A4.1.repro, TC7.inbox-gap). Build, M. PR #130 merged a gate after the audited commit; it is unaudited.
2. No notice on any dashboard page, CDS card or Decision Card; no acknowledgement before Try it; "synthetic only" not enforced (A4.2, A4.3a, A4.3b, A4.acc-2a, A4.acc-2b, P2.1, P4, S8). Build, M.
3. No synthetic-example label on any pack, and no spec field for it (A2.6a, A2.6b, P6.2, B21, LIC.open-4, TC11.gate1-label). Build, M.
4. The gate's secret scan has not been run as written: no trufflehog, not all branches and tags, no private report (A1.1b, A1.1d). Build, S. Rotation after it is the owner's (B08).
5. Which plan governs: private first, or public now (P7, TC10.public-timing). Owner.
6. NDA and pilot agreement, patent decision (B07, B04, LIC.patent). Owner, counsel.

**Before any partner signs in to the hosted site (inside stage 2)**

7. PHI mode `off` is the default and nothing refuses it with sign-in on; no scheduled purge; rows written under `off` cannot be erased; one route shape still returns legacy plaintext (A5.1a, A5.1b, A5.2a, A5.acc-2, A5.goal-legacy, TC6.plaintext). Build, L. Production keys and settings are the owner's (A5.1c).
8. No users or memberships; a sign-in without an organization claim lands in the shared `default` organization (A6.1a, A6.2b). Build, L.
9. No terms, privacy notice or acceptable-use text, and nothing records acceptance (B16). Counsel, then build, M.

**Before stage 3 (open-source launch)**

10. No NOTICE; CONTRIBUTING says "no separate agreement to sign" and no check enforces DCO sign-off (A1.4-NOTICE, A1.4-DCO-doc, A1.4-DCO-ci, A1.acc-3). Build, S.
11. Publishing is gated only by a GitHub setting the repository cannot show; `images.yml` still starts on any `v*` tag (A1.3b, A1.acc-2, B09c). Owner, plus a fail-closed guard, S.
12. No labelling workflow: nothing lets two clinicians label a case blind or sends a disagreement to a third (A8.1a, B19, B19.1, W2.clin-label). Build, L. No label in the repository comes from a clinician (A8.goal-3). Owner.
13. No minimum-evidence rule: "20/20" is shown as rank 1 (A9.pub-1). Build, S.
14. Where hidden-set content, labels and keys live is undecided; the plan runs A8 to A11 in the repository that will be opened (P6.3). Owner, then build.
15. `mendari eval --share` sends case ids (A11.2b-ids). Build, S.
16. Trademark filing, counsel sign-off, Vercel fork-preview protection (B05, B14, B23, B11, LIC.trademark-filing). Owner.

**Before stage 4 (scoreboards, registry, pack store)**

17. Pack patterns run on backtracking `re` with no time limit, a nested input schema can stall a decision with no pattern at all, and there is no decision budget; three of A3's four tests do not exist (A3.2a, A3.2c, A3.test-2, A3.acc-1, TC5.regex). Build, L. This moves to "before stage 3" if any outside pack is served, which `tasks/todo.md` plans.
18. No hidden set, lifecycle, grading role or protected store; no set has 100 positives for any of the 14 critical labels (A8.goal-1, A8.goal-2, A8.3, A8.3c, A8.3d, A8.acc-1, A9.data-1, B19.2, B34). Build, L; labelling is the owner's.
19. No grading method document (A9.gate-1, B30). Build with A9, M.
20. The registry serves the uploader's card, benchmark and leaderboard; no yank; a pulled pack can automate; no deploy configuration; tokens are per namespace, not per user (A10.1a, A10.acc-1, A10.x-yank, A10.x-auto, A10.5, A10.2a). Build, L.
21. No pack store, author role, dataset checks, PHI detector or promotion record (A7.1, A7.2, A7.3, A7.5, A7.acc-2, A7.acc-3, A7.prereq). Build, XL.
22. The share filter copies nested objects whole, so free text can leave (P2.2). Build, S.
23. The security-review packet lists the registry and Hub as out of scope (B29). Owner.

**Before stage 5 (self-service)**

24. No kill switch, runbook, status page or hosted monitoring schedule; the public health check stays green through a database outage (A13.1, A13.2, A13.3a, A13.3b, A13.acc-1, A13.acc-2, A13.acc-3). Build, L. Firewall enforcement, SOC 2, status-page provider and on-call are the owner's (A13.4a, B35, B36).

**Before stage 6 (clinical pilots)**

25. Site reports are unsigned, unvalidated and open to any token; suppression covers two fields only (A11.1a, A11.1b, A11.2b, A11.2-test, A11.3, A11.acc-1, A11.acc-3). Build, L.
26. CDS Hooks cannot be called by a real EHR: a signed token is refused with sign-in on and ignored in open mode; notes by link, codes by system and MedicationRequest are not read (A12.1a, A12.1b, A12.3a, A12.3b, A12.3d, A12.acc-2, A12.ctx-jwt-today). Build, XL. The vendor choice is the owner's (A12.acc-3).
27. Automation needs no local evidence and, at risk levels 0 and 1, no approval (B39, B42). Over an organization's quota even the local emergency rules are failed, so an emergency message loses its priority (A13.also-4). Build, M to L.
28. Clinician review of each pack, FDA opinion, site agreements (B21.1 to B21.5, B37). Owner.

### Where the plan itself is wrong, outdated or weaker than the code

These are the ones that change what should be built.

**Outdated since the plan was written**

1. **Pack safety is no longer "Unsafe" as described.** A pack cannot choose a secret or an endpoint since PR #127; the exploit is a permanent regression test (A3.1b, A3.test-1, TC5.secret). Correction: narrow A3 step 1 to what is left (a closed list of settable fields per provider type, a provider egress allow-list, the fuzz test).
2. **Counts and files.** The default selection is 2,121 tests (2,264 collected), not about 1,580. The 16 was right at the plan's commit as a count of checks (14 jobs); it is 17 now (15 jobs). LICENSE, the code of conduct, SECURITY.md and version 0.1.0 already exist. Correction: A1 step 4 becomes "add NOTICE, the disclosure window and DCO enforcement"; A2 step 1 drops "set version".
3. **"No accounts or organizations."** Organizations exist by identity-provider claim and decision data is scoped. Correction: A6 builds users, memberships and invitations on that tenancy and closes the `default` catch-all.
4. **The closed-source branches are moot.** Apache-2.0 is decided and already applied in the tree.

**Things the plan relies on that are not in the repository**

5. **The "Use Cases page" does not exist** in any tracked file, yet the shared header tells all thirteen prompts to update it. Under the header's own conflict rule each session must stop or invent one. Correction: "update `docs/`, the README, the `/docs` landing (`src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html`) and `CAPABILITIES` for any new FHIR surface."
6. **The launch-readiness report and EHR scorecard are not in the repository**, so "about 4 of 5" and "2.5 of 5" cannot be checked. Correction: commit both under `notes/` with their rubric, or replace the scores with checkable statements.
7. **Prompt ids collide.** `tasks/todo.md` already uses A1 to A11 and B1 to B10 for the community-packs plan. Correction: prefix the launch-plan prompts (this report uses LP-) and add one line to `tasks/todo.md` saying which plan governs.

**Sequence and gates**

8. **The label is a stage-2 prerequisite but sits in A2**, which runs eighth. Move it into the first safety prompt.
9. **A8 is in no gate**, though labelling cannot start without it, and it needs no accounts work. Split it and run the core before A6.
10. **The kill switch is gated at stage 5** but is needed the day the first partner signs in. Move it before stage 2.
11. **A3 gates stage 4, but outside packs arrive at stage 3** by pull request, and `tasks/todo.md` plans to serve them live. Pattern safety must gate whichever comes first.
12. **Terms and the privacy notice are in the stage-3 gate** but are needed before a partner signs in to the hosted site in stage 2. The table (page 5) and the figure (page 4) also disagree on whether A5 and A6 gate stage 2 or stage 3.
13. **A5 is sized "Days".** With the guard, the legacy rows, the cron route and a key ring it is 1.5 to 2.5 weeks. Split it.

**Safety wording**

14. **"AUTO-routed 7 of the 20 held-out emergencies it missed."** It missed 16 of 20 and automated 7 of the 16. And the gate does not fix the misses: with it on, all 16 still arrive at routine priority. Correction: state the goal as two checks (no configuration without a model in the safety screen produces AUTO; no critical case is automated on any shipped set), and tell reviewers when the emergency screen is keyword-only.
15. **"A rules-only deployment never produces AUTO"** is too narrow: the unsafe configurations include `router=jev` with a keyword screen, which every documented Jev setup uses.

**Statistics and grading (A8, A9, A11)**

16. **"Search the variants and report the best" selects on the test set.** Correction: split each frozen set by a hash of the case id into a selection part and a ranking part, or have the developer declare one configuration per arm before grading.
17. **"Not significant means a tie" does not define a ranking**: A can tie B, B tie C, and A beat C. Correction: safety floors first, then a lexicographic order on bands fixed in advance, with rank intervals shown.
18. **One hundred positives is a floor for evidence, not for a claim.** At 100 of 100 the exact 95% lower bound is 0.964; at 95 of 100 the interval is 0.887 to 0.984. A zero-miss lower bound of 0.97 needs 122 positives and 0.99 needs 368. Correction: publish the lower bound as the graded value and size each set from the bound the grade claims.
19. **The Holm family is undefined.** Correction: per pack and set, all arm pairs by all ranking criteria by all critical labels.
20. **"One re-run per version" does not bound submissions**, so a fixed hidden set leaks to repeated versions. Correction: a submission budget per organization and pack lineage per set rotation, banded results only, a sealed confirmation split, and a rotation rule. Page 1 calls the sets "rotating"; A8 and A9 never rotate them.
21. **"Byte-identical re-run" cannot hold for LLM arms.** Correction: statistics recomputed from a stored per-case ledger are byte-identical; remote arms run replicates and report the spread.
22. **ECE as a rank criterion.** Correction: rank on the Brier score; keep ECE as a description, mark it "not estimable" on small sets. The code today uses equal-width bins.
23. **False-automation rate has no stated denominator**, and the plan never ranks on the count that matters most. Correction: define false automation over all labelled cases, report AUTO error rate separately, and make "critical cases automated" a zero-tolerance gate.
24. **Cohen's kappa assumes the same two raters throughout.** With a pool of clinicians, use Krippendorff's alpha, and resample cases in the bootstrap.
25. **A public generator cannot produce hidden cases.** A8 lists "synthetic generator" as a source, but the generators and templates are in the tree that will be opened. And chaining every label in the audit trail would leak hidden content: chain keyed hashes.
26. **A dataset hash in a shared payload is a membership oracle for small sets**; summed counts ignore differences between sites; a chi-square test fails on small expected counts; sites can share only good results. Corrections: a keyed commitment, a random-effects estimate beside the pooled one, an exact or permutation test, and an evaluation ticket requested before the run.
27. **scipy and statsmodels are not dependencies.** Keep them out of the runtime: implement the exact methods in the engine and use scipy and statsmodels as development-only references in tests.

**Other prompts**

28. **A3's RE2 requirement is not a drop-in and is not enough.** Four shipped patterns use lookaround and would not compile; 53 use `\b` and 36 use `\w`, `\s` or `\d`, whose Unicode meaning differs in RE2; the schema validator runs patterns on `re` regardless; and a nested input schema stalls a decision with no pattern at all. Rewriting red-flag patterns changes digests and possibly emergency detection. The repository's notes say a second regex engine with a time budget was tried and dropped, without naming RE2. A CPU budget cannot be enforced in-thread on `re`. Correction: state the outcome (no pack-authored evaluation exceeds a fixed CPU bound; a blown budget sends the case to a person) and have the owner choose RE2 or an isolated worker before the build starts.
29. **A5 asks to "re-key" the input-hash key.** A keyed hash cannot be re-keyed without the input. Correction: a new key starts a new epoch; old hashes keep their key id.
30. **A7 promotes "through the existing governance approval".** That approval is permission to automate and is asked for only at risk levels 2 and 3. Promotion needs its own chained record. And "minimum 50 cases per label" would block every rare critical label; report the interval instead.
31. **A10 asks the registry to "recompute the risk level".** A risk level is declared, not computed; it can only be confirmed by a reviewer. The uploaded leaderboard and the site reports must be ignored like the card. Yank is named but never defined.
32. **A12's Inferno kits are not all aimed at a system like Mendari.** Name the suites, and use the Da Vinci CRD kit as the real CDS Hooks client.
33. **"Model-agnostic" needs a definition.** The fourth arm exists in no pack, the `llm` type has a default vendor model written into product code (P1.2, S5), and "+ Jev" names one vendor. Correction: define arms by provider class and remove the default model.
34. **No boundary between open and proprietary.** The plan keeps the grading service, scoreboards and site network proprietary, then tells A8 to A11 to build them in the repository that will be opened under Apache-2.0. Correction: a table saying, per component, open or closed and where it lives.
35. **security@ and conduct@ addresses.** The repository chose GitHub private vulnerability reporting and publishes no address, which avoids an unmonitored mailbox. Keep it; add only the 90-day window, and name a contact in the pilot agreement while the repository is private.
36. **Patent advice and Apache-2.0.** Section 3 of the licence grants every recipient a licence to the owner's patent claims that the released code practises. Tell the patent attorney the release is Apache-2.0 before deciding.
37. **The plan has no rule for the audit chain, grade freshness, release versioning or licence headers.** The Definition of shipped in Phase 4 adds them.

Every plan defect the auditors recorded, each with its evidence and a proposed correction, is in [`vision-audit-evidence.md`](vision-audit-evidence.md) under "Every plan defect, in full".

## Phase 3: Making Mendari more powerful

Twelve recommendations beyond closing the gaps, ranked by expected effect on the vision. Each cites the Phase 1 items it rests on. Effort: S under a day, M 1 to 3 days, L 1 to 2 weeks, XL more than 2 weeks.

### R1. One per-case ledger and one statistics module behind every number

- **Problem.** No function in `src/` computes an interval, a test or a resample (S4, A9.stat-1 to stat-5). Reports keep aggregates, the ids of missed critical cases and at most 50 misclassified cases, so a paired comparison of two versions or two arms cannot be computed afterwards (A9.stat-3). The pack store (A7.4), the scoreboard (A9) and the site network (A11.4b) each need the same statistics and would otherwise each write their own.
- **Proposal.** Every evaluation writes a content-addressed ledger: per case, a keyed hash of the case id, the truth, the predicted label and probabilities per question, the decision outcome, cost and latency. A new `mendari.evals.stats` module computes everything from ledgers: exact Clopper-Pearson intervals, exact McNemar, a seeded paired bootstrap, Holm adjustment. `eval`, `compare`, the Decision Card, the leaderboard, the pack store and the scoreboard all read from it. Ship it first on today's cards: "caught 20 of 20; with 95% confidence at least 83%".
- **Impact.** Largest single step toward credible grades, and it unblocks A7, A9 and A11. It also makes "byte-identical re-run" true for every arm, because statistics are recomputed from a stored ledger.
- **Risks.** Ledgers of hidden sets are sensitive: store ids as keyed hashes and keep hidden-set ledgers in the private store. Report format changes (bump to `mendari.eval/2`, keep reading version 1).
- **Effort.** L. **Confidence.** High.

### R2. Grades that are hard to game by construction

- **Problem.** As written, the plan selects the best variant on the ranking set, defines ties in a way that does not give an order, allows unlimited versions against a fixed hidden set, and never uses rotation (A9 plan defects; Phase 2 items 16 to 21).
- **Proposal.** Put five rules in the grading method before any grade is published: a selection part and a ranking part of every frozen set, split by a hash of the case id; a grade made of scoped attestations (met, not met, insufficient evidence), each on an interval bound, with no letter and no composite; a submission budget per organization and pack lineage per rotation, returning banded results only; a sealed confirmation split scored once before publication; a fixed share of each set rotated on a schedule, with a signed commitment (salted hash, counts) published at freeze so a later reveal can be checked.
- **Impact.** This is what a competitor who forks the code cannot copy, and what a health system will ask about first.
- **Risks.** Bands create edges; say so in the method. Budgets frustrate developers; the public development sets and the local loop (R6) are the answer.
- **Effort.** M for the method document, built with A9. **Confidence.** Moderate to high: the statistics are standard; the right budget and band widths need the first real sets.

### R3. Safety invariants enforced as gates, for every pack

- **Problem.** The inbox gap survived because nothing checks it: existing tests assert no automated critical case only on three of the pack's sets and skip the held-out one (A4.1c). The pack's own risk rationale says emergencies "are never auto-routed" while its card shows otherwise (B21.1, A4.1b.card).
- **Proposal.** Three mechanical checks. A parametrised test and a `reference-pack` CI step: for every pack on the search path, every configuration that can run offline, and every declared dataset, critical cases automated must be zero. A validation rule: when a slot answers a question named in `evaluation.critical` and its default variant is not a model, the pack must declare `automation_requires` for that slot. A card rule: a card may not state what its own benchmark refutes.
- **Impact.** Turns the plan's goal "no configuration can automate a missed emergency" from a one-off fix into a property of the platform, which is what a registry of outside packs needs.
- **Risks.** Thirteen test files use the inbox pack as the generic AUTO fixture and must be rewritten, not relaxed (A4.1a).
- **Effort.** M. **Confidence.** High.

### R4. An automation hold, and automation that needs local evidence

- **Problem.** There is no kill switch at any scope (A13.3b). At risk levels 0 and 1 a pack automates from the first request with no approval and no local evaluation (B39, B42). Over an organization's quota every provider is failed, including the free local rules that detect emergencies (A13.also-4).
- **Proposal.** A chained "automation hold" row scoped to the deployment, an organization, a pack or a digest, applied in `policy._cap` so it can only turn AUTO into HUMAN_REVIEW; a CLI break-glass that works when the API is down; the monitor may set a hold automatically on a seeded threshold. A pilot mode in which AUTO for a pack digest in an organization needs a stored local evaluation report and a governance approval at every risk level. Local rules providers are exempt from quota failure.
- **Impact.** Gives clinicians and IT a visible stop, and makes the plan's Part B rule ("validate on the site's own data before switching on automation") an engine rule instead of a contract clause.
- **Risks.** A hold stored in the database must be read per decision; cache it for seconds, not minutes.
- **Effort.** M for the hold, M for pilot mode. **Confidence.** High.

### R5. Bind every published number to the digest it was computed for

- **Problem.** 105 of 166 leaderboard rows were produced by an older version of the pack that carries them, and nothing marks them (A9.fresh-3, S10). The registry shows the uploader's card, benchmark and leaderboard as they are (A10.1a). The LLM provider records the requested model name, not the model that answered (A9.cfg-3).
- **Proposal.** Key each leaderboard row by pack digest, dataset hash and configuration; mark or drop rows whose digest is not the pack's; fail CI when a shipped card cites a stale benchmark. Render the card in the registry from the digested files only, and show two labelled groups on every pack page: "checked by the registry" and "claimed by the publisher". Record the provider-reported model on every answer.
- **Impact.** Makes the principle "grades are scoped and versioned" true for the numbers that exist today, before any hidden set exists.
- **Risks.** Stale rows disappear from cards until the owner re-runs the leaderboard with live keys.
- **Effort.** M. **Confidence.** High.

### R6. A faster, stricter local loop for pack authors

- **Problem.** The loop works (TC2.create to TC2.evaluate are Done) but has sharp edges found by running it: behaviour tests accept unknown `expect` keys and empty expectations, so a test can pass by accident; a provider override for an unknown slot silently does nothing; `validate` does not read the datasets a pack declares; `audit verify` can pass on the wrong file; several subcommands show tracebacks (A2.5a, A2.5c, A2.acc-3, and the opportunities recorded under A2). The default suite takes 202 s, not the "< 3 s" in `CLAUDE.md` (S1.gaps).
- **Proposal.** `mendari data check PACK [FILE]` running the A7.3 dataset checks locally and in the packs workflow; `mendari compare CURRENT DRAFT --dataset X` as a paired comparison that names the cases that changed; strict `expect` keys; an error for an unknown slot; one top-level error handler with `--debug`; `--version`; a fast test selection documented in `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Impact.** The create-improve-test loop is the product for developers at stage 3. These are the first things an outside author hits.
- **Risks.** Stricter test loading may fail existing community drafts; report every problem in one run.
- **Effort.** M. **Confidence.** High.

### R7. Decide the open and proprietary boundary, and make it physical

- **Problem.** There is no boundary today. The leaderboard, the registry, the Hub site and the share command all sit in the one tree that will be opened (P6.4, LIC.proprietary-boundary), and the plan tells A8 to A11 to build the proprietary parts there.
- **Proposal.** A table in the plan: for each component, open or closed, and where it lives. Open: the engine, the statistics module, the ledger format, the grading method, the verifier for signed grades. Closed and in a private repository with its own package name: the hidden-set store and its contents, the grading runner and its provider budget, the grading key, the submission ledger. Add a `TRADEMARKS.md`, a separate grading key, and a public tool that verifies a signed grade, so the mark rests on something checkable. Consider publishing from a fresh history (P6.7, A1.x-history).
- **Impact.** Keeps the method open, which is what earns trust, and the track record closed, which is what cannot be copied.
- **Risks.** Two repositories cost coordination. The legal parts (employer IP, the owner entity, CPT codes, AI-assisted authorship) are counsel's (LIC.employer-ip, LIC.copyright-holder, LIC.cpt).
- **Effort.** M to build, plus owner decisions. **Confidence.** Moderate.

### R8. One notice and plain words on every surface a clinician sees

- **Problem.** The notice exists on developer pages only, in four wordings (A4 plan defects, S8). The main result view prints raw codes for every pack by construction, and packs cannot declare display names (A4.4a, A4.acc-3). With the automation gate on, a keyword-only deployment still delivers 16 missed emergencies at routine priority with no hint to the reviewer (A4.1.repro).
- **Proposal.** One versioned notice defined once and carried in the decision payload itself, so the dashboard, CDS Hooks cards, the SMART launch view, FHIR Tasks and the Decision Card all show it. Display names for labels and questions in the spec, used everywhere a code is shown today. A queue banner and a per-item reason when the emergency screen is keyword-only.
- **Impact.** Clinician readiness is the row the plan scores lowest; this is the cheapest real movement on it.
- **Risks.** A spec revision changes every pack digest; do it once, with the label (see LP-02).
- **Effort.** M to L. **Confidence.** High.

### R9. A site network that resists selective and forged results

- **Problem.** Today a site is a free-text name, reports are unsigned, any publisher token can post under any site name, and the report's shape is not validated (A11.1a, A11.1b, A10.x-site-reports, A11.x-poison). Even with signatures, a site could share only its good runs, and nothing proves it ran the claimed pack (A11 plan defects).
- **Proposal.** Counts in, rates out: the payload carries integer counts only and the registry computes every rate. An evaluation ticket requested before the run, bound to site, pack digest, configuration and a keyed dataset commitment, so an unreported run is visible. A handful of public canary cases with each ticket as proof of execution. `--share-out payload.json` so a site's privacy office can read exactly what leaves before it is sent.
- **Impact.** Site evidence is the real-world half of every grade and the hardest part for a competitor to rebuild.
- **Risks.** Tickets add friction; keep an offline path. Canary cases prove execution, not honesty about the rest.
- **Effort.** L. **Confidence.** Moderate.

### R10. Seed the registry with packs that can be shared and recalled

- **Problem.** The prior-authorization pack is built on 15 AMA CPT codes (LIC.cpt). The registry has no yank or recall (A10.x-yank), runs no checks on publish, and a pulled pack can automate (A10.x-auto). `tasks/todo.md` plans to serve community packs live while patterns have no time limit (TC10.community).
- **Proposal.** Replace CPT codes with a fictional local code system and rebuild that pack. Add licence (SPDX), code systems used and review status as validated manifest fields. Run the existing pack gate on every registry publish in an isolated job. Treat yank as a recall: a signed record with a reason that `mendari pull` and a running server both honour. Record origin at pull and cap pulled packs at recommend-only until an organization approves the digest.
- **Impact.** A registry whose first five packs are clean, labelled and recallable sets the bar for every later one.
- **Risks.** Rebuilding a pack changes its data, model and benchmark; follow the pack-artifact rule in `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Effort.** L. **Confidence.** Moderate.

### R11. Lower the cost of the first EHR connection

- **Problem.** The sandbox data is shaped to fit the mappings: notes inline, the first coding only, `MedicationStatement`, the payer by identifier (A12.3a to A12.3d). On real EHR shapes the engine silently reads nothing, and a failed fetch fails the whole hook (A12.3f). The documented integration keys do not work as documented (A6.6a, A6.6b).
- **Proposal.** A data-coverage record on every decision: per mapped field, what was looked for and what was found, so "no notes read" is visible instead of silent. A second, vendor-shaped seeding mode for the sandbox (linked attachments, several codings, `MedicationRequest`). The Inferno Da Vinci CRD kit as the real CDS Hooks client in CI. Key presets that are the documentation: one table drives the API, the Keys screen and the guide.
- **Impact.** Moves EHR readiness with no vendor chosen, and gives an IT team a diagnosis instead of a wrong answer.
- **Risks.** The coverage record must hold counts and codes, never text.
- **Effort.** L. **Confidence.** Moderate.

### R12. Launch gates as a checked file

- **Problem.** Two plans disagree and neither is checked by anything (P7, TC10). The plan quotes counts that were stale within two days. Gate status had to be rebuilt by hand for this audit.
- **Proposal.** `launch/gates.yaml` listing every gate prerequisite with a probe: a test id, a file, a health field, or "owner attests" with a date. A small script prints the gate table and runs in CI. `tests/test_shipped.py` holds the Definition of shipped below as inventory-style tests. One line in `tasks/todo.md` names the governing plan.
- **Impact.** Stops a build session from walking past a gate, and makes the next audit a command.
- **Risks.** Owner attestations are only as good as the owner's care; keep them few and dated.
- **Effort.** S to M. **Confidence.** High.

## Phase 4: Shipping guideline and ready-made prompts

### Definition of shipped

Every change passes every line that applies before it merges. "Today" says whether the repository already enforces the line at the audited commit; where it does not, the prompt that adds the check is named.

**1. Tests and CI**

- [ ] `uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests` and `uv run mypy src tests` pass.
- [ ] `uv run pytest` passes on SQLite, and again with `MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL` set to the project's own Postgres container. A local run without Postgres skips 270 tests and is not CI's run.
- [ ] `uv run pytest -m browser` passes when a dashboard file changed.
- [ ] Every new behaviour has a test that fails without the change. A changed safety rule has a test for the unsafe case.
- [ ] Every `ci.yml` job is green on the pull request, not only the required ones. Today: which checks are required is a GitHub setting the repository cannot show (S1.required).

**2. Real computation, no stubs**

- [ ] No mock, stub, fake or placeholder in `src/mendari` outside `mendari.sandbox`. Fakes live in `tests/` and enter through a constructor argument. Today: convention only (S3); LP-03 adds the test.
- [ ] No metric, interval or card value is typed in by hand. Each is computed from data named in the output.
- [ ] Randomness is seeded and the seed is written in the output. Two runs with the same inputs give the same bytes for everything except a separate `run` block (id, time, latency).

**3. Statistics with confidence intervals**

- [ ] Every proportion shown to a user appears as k of n with its exact Clopper-Pearson 95% interval. No bare percentage.
- [ ] Every comparison of two versions or two arms is paired on the same cases: exact McNemar for proportions, a seeded paired bootstrap (2,000 resamples) for Brier and other means, Holm-adjusted over a family written in the output.
- [ ] A figure on fewer cases than the method's minimum is shown as "insufficient evidence", never as a number alone.
- [ ] Each new statistical function has a fixture test against a value computed independently with scipy or statsmodels (development dependencies only). Today: no statistics exist (S4); LP-10 adds them.

**4. Model-agnostic behaviour**

- [ ] No vendor or model name in product code outside `src/mendari/providers/`, and no default model. Today: `DEFAULT_MODEL` in `src/mendari/providers/llm.py:50` breaks this (P1.2, S5.default).
- [ ] A new provider implements the `Provider` interface in `src/mendari/providers/base.py` and raises only `ProviderError`.
- [ ] Anything scored is scored in every arm the pack supports; an unsupported arm shows "not applicable".
- [ ] The model that actually answered is recorded per decision and per report.

**5. Audit-chain coverage**

- [ ] Every new state-changing route or command appends a chained event holding ids and hashes only, never free text.
- [ ] The event is bound to its organization.
- [ ] `mendari audit verify` passes after the new operation, on SQLite and Postgres, in a test.
- [ ] The route is added to the route inventory test. Today: the chain is tested, but several writes are unchained (S6.coverage).

**6. Patient-data handling**

- [ ] No real PHI in code, tests, fixtures, logs or examples. Test data comes from `mendari.sandbox.synthetic_*`.
- [ ] New stored fields that can hold input text are sealed in `encrypt` mode, absent in `hash` mode, and covered by retention purge.
- [ ] A route that returns stored input needs the reviewer or admin role and chains the read.
- [ ] Reports, exports, logs and shared payloads hold ids and counts, never case text. A closed schema enforces it where data leaves the deployment.
- [ ] Nothing new is sent to a third-party provider without appearing in the pack's declared state fields.

**7. Safety notices**

- [ ] Every new screen, card, launch view or published page carries the versioned notice from the single source. Today: no single source (S8); LP-05 adds it.
- [ ] No wording implies clinical validity. A pack that is not clinician-reviewed shows the synthetic-example label.
- [ ] Policy changes only lower automation. No change lets a configuration produce AUTO that could not before, unless a test shows no critical case is automated on every declared dataset.

**8. Docs and capability statement**

- [ ] `docs/`, `README.md`, the `/docs` landing and its snippets describe the new behaviour; `uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py` passes and `mkdocs build --strict` passes in CI.
- [ ] A new FHIR surface has a `CAPABILITIES` entry in `src/mendari/standards/capability.py`.
- [ ] A spec change is written in a new spec document and older packs still load.
- [ ] Security-relevant behaviour is reflected in `docs/security/threat-model.md` and its test.

**9. Effect on the scoreboard and grades**

- [ ] If a pack, a generator, a rule or policy code changed: `./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh` was rerun and the data, model, benchmark and Decision Card are committed together.
- [ ] No card or leaderboard row cites a benchmark computed for a different pack digest. Today: 105 of 166 rows do (A9.fresh-3); LP-10 adds the check.
- [ ] A change to a metric, an interval or the ranking bumps the report format or the grading-method version, and says in the pull request which published results become stale.
- [ ] The pull request states the effect on each reference pack's critical-label figures, or "none".

**10. Licensing**

- [ ] New source files carry `# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0`. Today: 0 of 229 Python files do and nothing checks (S11); LP-13 adds the check.
- [ ] A new dependency is on the licence allow-list; a new vendored asset is in NOTICE with its licence file beside it.
- [ ] No third-party clinical content with its own terms (CPT, SNOMED CT, and so on) is added without a NOTICE entry and counsel's agreement.
- [ ] Every commit is signed off (`git commit -s`).

**11. Reversible and scoped**

- [ ] Database changes are additive. A stored record or pack written before the change still loads and verifies.
- [ ] The diff touches only what the change needs.

### Ready-made prompts for Claude Opus 5.5

Twenty-three prompts in priority order. Each is one Claude Code session on a new branch from `main`, and each stands alone: paste the whole block.

Ids start with LP- because `tasks/todo.md` already uses A1 to A11 for another plan. Each block opens with the plan's shared header, unchanged, followed by four corrections from this audit.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| LP-02 | Secret scan as the gate requires | A1 step 1 | owner names the report path | Stage 2 |

| LP-04 | Pack review status and display names | A2 step 6, A4 step 4 | LP-01 | Stage 2 |
| LP-05 | One notice, acknowledgement, plain-language results | A4 steps 2 to 5 | LP-04 | Stage 2 |

| LP-07 | Shared reports carry counts only | new, from A11 | none | Stage 3 |
| LP-08 | Hosted data protection | A5 steps 1, 2, 5 | owner sets keys | Stage 2 hosted access |
| LP-09 | Key rotation | A5 steps 3, 4 | LP-08 | Stage 3 |
| LP-10 | Statistics module and per-case ledger | new | none | LP-11, 17, 20, 22 |
| LP-11 | Evaluation-set store and blind labelling | A8 | LP-10; owner decides where hidden data lives | Stage 2 labelling |
| LP-12 | Accounts, memberships, invitations | A6 | LP-08 | Stage 2 hosted access |

Run order: LP-01, LP-02 and LP-03 first, in any order. Then LP-04, LP-05, LP-06, LP-07. Then LP-08 and LP-10 in parallel, then LP-11, LP-09, LP-12. Then LP-13 to LP-16. Then LP-17 to LP-20. Then LP-21, LP-22, LP-23.

#### LP-01. Inbox automation gate and the invariant for every pack

Amends A4 step 1. This is the full revised text of that step.

```text
You are working in the Mendari repository on a new branch from main.
Before changing anything, read the project instructions file, docs/spec/decision-pack-0.2.md, docs/guides/authoring-a-pack.md and tasks/todo.md.
Non-negotiable rules:
- No mocks, stubs or placeholder calculations in product code. Every computation and statistic is real and deterministic (seeded where random).
- Every change ships with tests. The full suite, browser tests and all CI jobs stay green. Add new CI jobs where this prompt asks.
- Update the docs, the generated capability statement and the Use Cases page for anything that changes behaviour.
- Synthetic data only. Never touch production or the hosted deployment.
- Never weaken an existing safety rule, cap or check to make a test pass.
- If something in this prompt conflicts with the code or is unsafe, stop and write the conflict to tasks/todo.md instead of guessing.
Finish with: what changed, the test evidence (commands and counts), anything not done, and open questions.

Corrections to the header above, from the audit of commit e55ed48c (notes/vision-audit.md):
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.
- This prompt's id starts with LP-. tasks/todo.md uses A1-A11 and B1-B10 for a different plan; do not confuse them.
- The change must pass the "Definition of shipped" in notes/vision-audit.md, Phase 4.
- Local checks: uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests; uv run mypy src tests; uv run pytest (about 200 s) on SQLite and again with MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL set to Mendari's own Postgres container; uv run pytest -m browser; uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py; ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh when a pack, a generator or policy code changes.

LP-01. Inbox automation gate and the safety invariant for every pack (amends A4 step 1)

Goal: no configuration without a model in the safety screen can produce AUTO, for any pack, and a test proves it.

Context:
- packs/patient-inbox-router/policy.yaml has no automation_requires. The pack is risk level 0 and its default safety_screen is keyword rules. On data/red_flags_holdout (30 cases, 20 emergencies) the default configuration detects 4, misses 16, and sends 7 of the 16 to AUTO as a routine nurse message.
- The engine already supports automation_requires: src/mendari/core/policy.py (apply_policy, _cap) and src/mendari/core/card.py. referral-router, prior-auth-preflight and ai-guard use it.
- First check whether branch claude/inbox-automation-gate has merged. If it has, audit it against the requirements below and add only what is missing.

Requirements:
1. Add automation_requires to the inbox pack so AUTO needs safety_screen in {rules_and_jev, rules_and_llm}.
2. Correct the pack's risk rationale and known_failure_modes in pack.yaml: they say emergencies are never auto-routed. Say what is true with the gate on: missed emergencies reach a person, at routine priority.
3. When automation_requires is unmet, add a plain-language reason to the decision ("Emergency screen is keyword-only") so the review queue can show it.
4. Bump the pack version, update CHANGELOG.md, run ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh, and commit data, model, benchmark and Decision Card together. Drop or visibly mark leaderboard rows computed for the old digest; do not re-run remote providers.
5. Fix every documented Jev or LLM recipe (README.md, docs/, packs/patient-inbox-router) that sets only router: each must set the safety screen too.
6. Pack validation (src/mendari/core/spec.py, load_pack in src/mendari/core/pack.py): when a slot answers a question named in evaluation.critical and its default variant is not a model, the pack must declare automation_requires for that slot. Apply it to new spec versions only if it would reject a pack that loads today.

Tests:
- For the inbox pack: every configuration whose safety_screen is outside the allowed set, on every declared dataset including red_flags_holdout, produces zero AUTO.
- For every pack on the search path, every configuration that runs offline, every declared dataset: critical cases with outcome AUTO equal zero. Add the same check as a step in the reference-pack CI job.
- The seven cases rfh-u04, u09, u10, u11, u12, u18, u19 are HUMAN_REVIEW with the new reason.
- Tests that used the inbox pack as the generic AUTO fixture keep their AUTO coverage through an explicit model-screen fake in tests/.

Acceptance: both invariant tests pass on SQLite and Postgres; the reference-pack job fails if any shipped default automates a critical case; mendari test passes on all five packs.

Do not:
- Do not relax or delete a test that now fails because the inbox no longer automates. Rewrite it to run a model-screen configuration.
- Do not raise recall by editing the held-out cases or the keyword list to fit them.
- Do not claim the gate fixes detection. It stops automation; the 16 misses remain.
```

```text
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.

LP-02. Secret scan of the whole history (amends A1 step 1)

Goal: before any design partner gets repository access, the owner holds one private report of every secret in every ref.

Context:
- The ci.yml "secrets" job runs gitleaks v8.30.1 on the checked-out history only, with one allowance for deploy/onprem/.env.example. Nothing runs trufflehog. No report exists.
- The owner supplies REPORT_PATH, a directory outside the repository. If it is not given, stop and ask.

Requirements:
1. Make a mirror clone in a temporary directory outside the repository and fetch refs/pull/* as well as all branches and tags.
2. Run gitleaks (the version CI pins) and trufflehog (a pinned release, git mode, verified and unverified results) over the mirror. Use redaction flags. Never print a secret value to the terminal or a log.
3. Write REPORT_PATH/secret-scan-<date>.md: per finding, the detector, the commit, the file and line, the ref that reaches it, and which service it belongs to. Values redacted. Do not rotate anything.
4. In the repository: add a weekly scheduled run and a tag run of the secrets job, and a positive-control test that a throwaway repository holding a fake key pattern fails the scan.
5. Record in tasks/todo.md that the scan ran, its date and tool versions, and the count of findings. No locations and no values in the repository.

Tests: the positive-control test; tests/test_workflow_safety.py still passes.

Acceptance: the report exists at REPORT_PATH and covers branches, tags and pull-request refs; the scheduled job is in ci.yml; nothing secret is in the diff.

Do not:
- Do not write the report, a finding or a value anywhere inside the repository.
- Do not rewrite history. Whether to publish from a fresh history is the owner's decision (LP-13).
- Do not install tools into the project environment; use pinned release binaries in a temporary directory.
```

```text
LP-03. Launch gates as a checked file, and tests for the Definition of shipped (new)

Goal: a build session cannot walk past a launch gate, and the shipping rules are checked by tests instead of by reading.

Context:
- Two plans disagree: the launch plan (private partners first) and tasks/todo.md's "Going public: owner steps". The owner decides which governs. If tasks/todo.md does not say, stop and ask.
- notes/vision-audit.md lists each gate's prerequisites and the Definition of shipped.

Requirements:
1. launch/gates.yaml: the five gates, each prerequisite with an id, an owner (build or owner) and a probe. A probe is a test id, a file that must exist, a setting, or "attested" with a name and date filled in by the owner.
2. scripts/check_gates.py prints a table of gates and prerequisites with met or not met, and exits non-zero when asked to assert a gate that is not met. Run it in CI in report mode.
3. One line at the top of tasks/todo.md naming the governing plan. Rename the launch-plan prompts there with the LP- prefix.
4. tests/test_shipped.py, inventory style, one test per rule:
   - no unittest.mock, Mock, Stub or Fake class and no TODO or FIXME under src/mendari outside mendari.sandbox;
   - no vendor model name in src/mendari outside src/mendari/providers (list the allowed exceptions explicitly);
   - a route inventory: every state-changing route in the app is listed with the chained event it appends, or with a written reason it appends none; a new route missing from the list fails;
   - pyproject.toml and mendari.__version__ agree;
   - every tracked source file has an SPDX line (mark expected failure until LP-13 adds the headers).
5. Correct CLAUDE.md: the default suite takes about 200 seconds, and document a fast selection for the edit loop.

Tests: the new test file; a unit test of check_gates.py on a fixture gates file.

Acceptance: check_gates.py reproduces the gate table in notes/vision-audit.md at this commit; test_shipped.py passes or marks the known gaps as expected failures with the prompt id that closes each.

Do not:
- Do not mark an owner prerequisite met. Only the owner fills in an attestation.
- Do not make the gate check block ordinary pull requests; it reports, and asserts only when asked.
```

```text
LP-04. Pack review status and display names, in one spec revision (amends A2 step 6 and A4 step 4)

Goal: every reference pack says "Synthetic example. Not clinically reviewed. Not for patient care." on every surface, from one structured field, and every answer has a name a clinician can read.

Context:
- No pack carries the label and the spec has no field for it. Publisher, Card and PackManifest in src/mendari/core/spec.py are strict models that refuse extra keys.
- A Question has type, instructions, criteria, levels and derive only, so packs cannot declare display names.
- Both changes alter every pack digest, so do them together and rebuild the packs once.

Requirements:
1. A new spec document docs/spec/decision-pack-0.3.md. Packs written for 0.1 and 0.2 still load unchanged.
2. A review block on the manifest: status is synthetic_example or clinician_reviewed. clinician_reviewed needs reviewer role, date and the digest reviewed, and is void for any other digest. A data-provenance field: synthetic, hand-written, or de-identified real.
3. Optional display names for each question and each label, with a title-cased fallback so older packs still render.
4. The platform derives the sentence from the field; a pack never types it. render_card in src/mendari/core/card.py prints it as the first line under the title. The dashboard pack view, the Try it result, the Hub pack page, CDS Hooks cards and the README pack table show it.
5. Set status synthetic_example and display names on the five packs. Rerun ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh and commit the regenerated artifacts together.

Tests:
- Each of the five packs, on each surface (manifest, Decision Card, dashboard, docs), shows the exact sentence.
- A pack with clinician_reviewed for digest X and content digest Y shows the synthetic label.
- A 0.2 pack with no review block loads and shows the label by default.
- Browser test: the dashboard pack view shows the label.

Acceptance: 5 packs by 4 surfaces all show the sentence; mendari test passes on all five; older spec fixtures still load.

Do not:
- Do not put the sentence in free text inside pack.yaml.
- Do not let a pack mark itself reviewed without the reviewer fields and a matching digest.
- Do not change pack behaviour: the five packs must decide their examples exactly as before.
```

```text
LP-05. One notice on every surface, a recorded acknowledgement, and a plain-language result view (amends A4 steps 2 to 5)

Goal: every screen and card says what Mendari is, nobody uses Try it without acknowledging the synthetic-data rule, and the main result shows names, not codes.

Context:
- The plan's notice text is nowhere in the tree. The dashboard shows only a static badge. Notices exist on developer pages in four wordings.

2. Render it from that source on: every dashboard route including the launch-only shell and an open drawer, at every width; /docs and /docs/reference; the Hub layout; the Decision Card header; CDS Hooks cards; the SMART launch view. Add a notice field to the decision payload so API and FHIR consumers receive it.
3. Acknowledgement: a blocking dialog before the Try it form is usable, stating that only synthetic data may be entered. The server refuses a dashboard-session decide without a current acknowledgement. Chain notice.acknowledged with the notice version and the principal's stable id. A new notice version asks again.
4. A deployment data-policy setting (synthetic or phi). The dashboard badge follows it instead of static HTML.
5. Main result view: display names for the recommendation, labels and questions. Move thresholds, provider settings and raw codes into the audit record drawer.
6. Vendor axe-core (pinned, checksum recorded) and run it in the browser tests with tags wcag2a, wcag2aa, wcag21aa and wcag22aa on every view, in both themes. Style with tokens only.

Tests:
- Browser: one parametrised test over every hash route, /docs and an open drawer asserts the notice text is visible, on desktop and phone widths.
- Browser: Decide is unreachable before acknowledging; exactly one chained event exists after; a version bump asks again.
- API: a dashboard-session decide without acknowledgement is refused; an API key decide is unaffected.
- No raw code appears in the main result view of any reference pack.
- axe reports no violations on the new and changed views.
- mendari audit verify passes after acknowledgements, on SQLite and Postgres.

Acceptance: the notice is on every route and every card type; the gate is enforced by the server, not only the page; the axe run is in CI.

Do not:
- Do not write the notice text in more than one place.
- Do not store the acknowledgement only in the browser.
- Do not put free text in the chained event.
- Do not use hex colours, raw spacing or z-index values; tests/test_dashboard_contrast.py must pass.
```

```text
LP-06. Automation hold: one switch that forces human review (new; replaces the kill switch in A13 step 3)

Goal: a named person can stop automation for one pack, one organization or the whole deployment in seconds, without a redeploy, and it is tested end to end.

Context:
- No kill switch exists. apply_policy in src/mendari/core/policy.py takes the manifest, answers, failures, a governance override and provider variants. _cap can only turn AUTO into HUMAN_REVIEW.
- Governance approvals are stored per organization, chained and looked up per decision through governance_source in src/mendari/core/decision.py. Reuse that pattern.
- An environment variable is not acceptable: on a serverless host it needs a redeploy and warm instances keep the old value.

Requirements:
1. A hold is a database row: scope (deployment, organization, pack or pack digest), who set it, a reason code, when. Setting and lifting are chained events.
2. Each decision looks up holds (cached for a few seconds at most) and passes them to policy. In _cap a hold turns AUTO into HUMAN_REVIEW and keeps the recommendation, priority and reasons. It never touches ABSTAIN or BLOCK and never raises automation.
3. Routes for admins to set, list and lift holds; a dashboard control; the state shown in the signed-in /api/health answer and on the pack view.
4. CLI: mendari automation hold [--all | --org ORG | --pack ID] and mendari automation release, working directly on the database when the API is down.
5. Local rules providers are exempt from quota failure, so an organization over quota still gets its keyword emergency rules.
6. docs/operations/automation-hold.md: when to use it and how.

Tests:
- For each entry point (REST decide, FHIR decide, CDS Hooks, SMART decide): with a hold on, a case that would be AUTO is HUMAN_REVIEW; after release it is AUTO again.
- A hold on organization A does not affect organization B.
- A property test: for any decision, the outcome with a hold is never more automated than without.
- mendari audit verify passes after set and release, on SQLite and Postgres.

Acceptance: the end-to-end test passes over HTTP on both databases; the CLI works with the server stopped.

Do not:
- Do not implement the hold as an environment variable or a setting read at start-up.
- Do not let a hold change a recommendation, only the outcome.
- Do not cache hold state longer than a few seconds.
```

```text
LP-07. Shared evaluation reports carry counts only (new; brings part of A11 forward)

Goal: nothing that identifies a case can leave a deployment through mendari eval --share, and the registry refuses anything else.

Context:
- shareable() in src/mendari/evals/share.py is a top-level key allow-list that copies nested objects whole. It sends missed_ids (the dataset's record ids) whenever a critical case is missed. MIN_GROUP = 10 is applied to subgroup cells and calibration bins only.
- The registry (src/mendari/hub/registry.py) accepts report: dict[str, Any], inserts the row before computing the summary, ignores unknown fields, and lets any publisher token post under any site name. The Hub shows these on the pack page.

Requirements:
1. Define mendari.site-report/1 as pydantic models with extra="forbid", strict integers, and strings limited to enumerated values taken from the published pack (question names, labels, declared subgroup values). No free-text field, no ids, no paths, no hostnames.
2. The payload holds integer counts only: totals, confusion-matrix cells, per-critical-label n, detected and automated, calibration bin counts, outcome counts. The receiver computes every rate.
3. Rebuild the critical entries from counts and drop missed_ids.
4. Use the same models on the sender and the receiver. The registry validates before any insert and answers 422 for anything else.
5. Until site signatures exist (LP-22), the registry stores such reports as unverified, and the Hub does not show them on pack pages.
6. mendari eval --share-out FILE writes the exact payload and a readable summary without sending.
7. Correct the docs that describe sharing.

Tests:
- A fixture report with a missed critical case: the payload contains no record id.
- Parametrised: one unknown key at every nesting level, and one over-long or free-text string in every string position, each rejected by the sender and by the registry.
- A malformed report (a list where an object belongs) gets 422 and inserts nothing.
- The Hub pack page shows no unverified site report.

Acceptance: a payload containing any case text or id fails on both sides; existing share tests pass or are replaced by stricter ones.

Do not:
- Do not keep a permissive fallback for old report shapes.
- Do not build site keys, signatures or pooling here; that is LP-22.
- Do not send a dataset hash; LP-22 replaces it with a keyed commitment.
```

```text
LP-08. Patient-data protection on the hosted site, part 1 (amends A5 steps 1, 2 and 5)

Goal: a signed-in or hosted deployment cannot start in a mode that stores inputs it cannot protect or erase, and stored inputs expire on a schedule.

Context:
- MENDARI_PHI_MODE defaults to off; all five packs set store_input: full; in that combination the input sits inside the hash-chained decision record. create_app in src/mendari/server/app.py reads sign-in settings and the PHI policy and never relates them. src/mendari/server/vercel.py requires no mode.
- vercel.json has no crons. purge_inputs in src/mendari/core/audit.py is called only from the CLI. mendari phi purge --days 0 exits with an error.
- Rows written under off keep their input after the mode changes, and removing it breaks audit verify.
- The owner generates the keys and sets production variables; never touch the hosted deployment.

Requirements:
0. Legacy rows: in hash and encrypt modes, blank a record's own input and evidence for any caller who is not a reviewer or admin, and chain each read by one who is. Write the owner's choice in docs/deploying-with-phi.md: retire the old database, or keep it read-gated.
1. Start-up guard in create_app: refuse mode off when sign-in is on or when VERCEL is set, with one error line naming the setting and the fix. Open-mode local sandboxes may use off. Give tests/conftest.py a helper that sets encrypt-mode test keys for signed-in fixtures.
2. On the hosted entrypoint the default is encrypt; hash is allowed.
3. A cron route (for example GET /api/internal/retention), listed in _RULES in src/mendari/server/auth.py, that checks a CRON_SECRET bearer, purges in bounded batches, logs a summary and chains it. Add it to vercel.json crons. Retention days in settings, default 30. Report retention days and the last purge in the signed-in health answer.
4. mendari phi purge: --days 0 or --all purges everything, with an explicit confirmation flag.
5. Erase one decision's kept input by id, chained, as notes erase already does.

Tests:
- The guard: sign-in on with mode off refuses to start; VERCEL set with mode off refuses; open local mode off starts.
- Purge, purge-all and erase leave mendari audit verify passing, on SQLite and Postgres.
- For every mounted route and every role: in encrypt mode no response contains stored plaintext unless the caller is a signed-in reviewer or admin, and a chained read event exists. Include rows written earlier under off.
- The cron route refuses a request without the secret.

Acceptance: all of the above in CI; docs/deploying-with-phi.md and docker-compose.yml match the new rules.

Do not:
- Do not edit or delete chained records to remove legacy inputs.
- Do not let a machine credential or open mode read sealed inputs.
- Do not weaken the guard to keep an existing test green; move the test to an explicit mode.
- Do not put a key, a secret or a production value in the repository.
```

```text
Finish with: what changed, the test evidence (commands and counts), anything not done, and open questions.

Corrections to the header above, from the audit of commit e55ed48c (notes/vision-audit.md):
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.
- This prompt's id starts with LP-. tasks/todo.md uses A1-A11 and B1-B10 for a different plan; do not confuse them.
- The change must pass the "Definition of shipped" in notes/vision-audit.md, Phase 4.
- Local checks: uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests; uv run mypy src tests; uv run pytest (about 200 s) on SQLite and again with MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL set to Mendari's own Postgres container; uv run pytest -m browser; uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py; ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh when a pack, a generator or policy code changes.

LP-09. Key rotation (amends A5 steps 3 and 4)

Goal: the PHI key, the input-hash key and API keys can each be rotated without losing data or breaking the audit chain.

Context:
- A ciphertext is base64(nonce + AES-GCM output) with no key id; the policy in src/mendari/core/phi.py holds exactly one key. There is no rotation code.
- A keyed hash cannot be re-keyed without the input, so the plan's "re-key in batches" does not apply to the input-hash key.
- Requires LP-08.

Requirements:
1. PHI key: a key ring with a key id stored in each ciphertext. mendari phi rotate re-encrypts in batches; each batch is a chained event; rows without a key id are read with the first key.
2. Input-hash key: a new key starts a new epoch. Each stored hash records its key id. Old hashes are kept, and matching across epochs is not attempted. Say so in the docs.
3. API keys: rotate creates a new key that overlaps the old one for a set period, then the old one is revoked. Route, store method and chained events here; the screen is LP-12.
4. An interrupted rotation resumes safely.

Tests: rotation of 1,000 rows in batches leaves every input readable and mendari audit verify passing, on SQLite and Postgres; an interrupted run resumes; an old API key works inside the overlap and is refused after it.

Acceptance: the three rotations are tested and documented in docs/deploying-with-phi.md.

Do not:
- Do not rewrite chained records.
- Do not log a key or a key fragment.
- Do not claim hashes were re-keyed.
```

#### LP-10. Statistics module and per-case ledger

New work (recommendations R1 and R5). The foundation for LP-11, LP-17, LP-20 and LP-22.

```text

Before changing anything, read the project instructions file, docs/spec/decision-pack-0.2.md, docs/guides/authoring-a-pack.md and tasks/todo.md.
Non-negotiable rules:
- No mocks, stubs or placeholder calculations in product code. Every computation and statistic is real and deterministic (seeded where random).
- Every change ships with tests. The full suite, browser tests and all CI jobs stay green. Add new CI jobs where this prompt asks.
```
