```text
Finish with: what changed, the test evidence (commands and counts), anything not done, and open questions.

Corrections to the header above, from the audit of commit e55ed48c (notes/vision-audit.md):
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.
- This prompt's id starts with LP-. tasks/todo.md uses A1-A11 and B1-B10 for a different plan; do not confuse them.
- The change must pass the "Definition of shipped" in notes/vision-audit.md, Phase 4.
- Local checks: uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests; uv run mypy src tests; uv run pytest (about 200 s) on SQLite and again with MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL set to Mendari's own Postgres container; uv run pytest -m browser; uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py; ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh when a pack, a generator or policy code changes.

LP-10. A statistics module and a per-case ledger behind every number (new)

Goal: every figure Mendari shows has an exact interval, every comparison is paired, and both are recomputed byte-identically from a stored ledger.

Context:
- src/mendari/evals/metrics.py and harness.py (build_report) compute point estimates only. Reports keep aggregates, missed ids and at most 50 misclassified cases. ECE uses 10 equal-width bins.
- Leaderboard rows are not tied to a pack digest: 105 of 166 shipped rows come from an older pack version. The Decision Card and CLI sort by accuracy alone. With 20 positives a card shows "20/20" in first place.
- scipy, statsmodels and numpy are not dependencies.

Requirements:
1. src/mendari/evals/stats.py, pure Python, no new runtime dependency:
   - clopper_pearson(k, n, level=0.95): exact, from the beta quantile, implemented with a documented numerical method;
   - mcnemar_exact(b, c): two-sided exact binomial on discordant pairs;
   - paired_bootstrap(a, b, statistic, resamples=2000, seed): percentile interval of the difference, resampling cases;
   - bootstrap(values, statistic, resamples=2000, seed);
   - holm(p_values, alpha=0.05).
2. A ledger per evaluation run: per case, a keyed hash of the case id, the truth and predicted label and probabilities per question, the decision outcome, whether the case is critical-positive, cost and latency. Content-addressed. No input text.
3. Report format mendari.eval/2: a deterministic result block computed only from the ledger and seeds, and a separate run block (id, created_at, latency). Version 1 reports still load.
4. Every proportion in reports, cards, the dashboard and the CLI is shown as k of n with its Clopper-Pearson interval: accuracy, each critical label's detection, false automation over all labelled cases, automation rate. Brier with a bootstrap interval. Keep the equal-width bins table and add an equal-mass ECE as a new field, marked "not estimable" under a minimum bin size.
5. Evidence rule: a critical label with fewer than 100 positives shows "insufficient evidence" beside its figure and takes no rank.
6. mendari compare A B --dataset X: a paired comparison from two ledgers on the same cases, naming the cases that changed.
7. Leaderboard rows keyed by pack digest, dataset hash and configuration. Rows for another digest are marked "earlier version" and take no rank. One ranking function in Python, written into the data, used by the card, the CLI and the dashboard: critical cases caught first, never accuracy alone.
8. Record the provider-reported model on each answer and in each report.
9. Remove DEFAULT_MODEL from src/mendari/providers/llm.py: an llm variant with no model configured fails as not_configured.

Tests:
- Fixture tests of every function against scipy.stats and statsmodels, added to the dev dependency group only: exact methods to 1e-12; bootstrap by feeding both implementations the same index matrix.
- Known values: 20 of 20 gives a lower bound of 0.8316; 95 of 100 gives 0.8872 to 0.9836; 100 of 100 gives a lower bound of 0.9638.
- Two runs with the same inputs and seed give byte-identical result blocks.
- A pack with 20 emergency positives shows "insufficient evidence".
- A leaderboard row whose digest differs from the pack's is marked and unranked.

Acceptance: no bare percentage remains on a card, in the CLI or on the dashboard; the reference-pack job compares the deterministic result block byte for byte; all five packs are rebuilt and committed.

Do not:
- Do not add scipy, numpy or statsmodels as runtime dependencies.
- Do not use a normal approximation for a proportion.
- Do not store input text or raw case ids in a ledger.
- Do not change a metric's definition silently: bump the format and list what becomes stale.
```

#### LP-11. Evaluation-set store and blind labelling

Amends A8. Full revised text.

```text
You are working in the Mendari repository on a new branch from main.
Before changing anything, read the project instructions file, docs/spec/decision-pack-0.2.md, docs/guides/authoring-a-pack.md and tasks/todo.md.
Non-negotiable rules:
- No mocks, stubs or placeholder calculations in product code. Every computation and statistic is real and deterministic (seeded where random).
- Every change ships with tests. The full suite, browser tests and all CI jobs stay green. Add new CI jobs where this prompt asks.
- Update the docs, the generated capability statement and the Use Cases page for anything that changes behaviour.
- Synthetic data only. Never touch production or the hosted deployment.
- Never weaken an existing safety rule, cap or check to make a test pass.
- If something in this prompt conflicts with the code or is unsafe, stop and write the conflict to tasks/todo.md instead of guessing.
Finish with: what changed, the test evidence (commands and counts), anything not done, and open questions.

Corrections to the header above, from the audit of commit e55ed48c (notes/vision-audit.md):
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.
- This prompt's id starts with LP-. tasks/todo.md uses A1-A11 and B1-B10 for a different plan; do not confuse them.
- The change must pass the "Definition of shipped" in notes/vision-audit.md, Phase 4.
- Local checks: uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests; uv run mypy src tests; uv run pytest (about 200 s) on SQLite and again with MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL set to Mendari's own Postgres container; uv run pytest -m browser; uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py; ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh when a pack, a generator or policy code changes.

LP-11. Evaluation-set store, blind clinician labelling and agreement (amends A8)

Goal: hidden held-out sets that are labelled by clinicians blind, with measured agreement, and that no route, export or log can leak.

Context:
- No evaluation-set entity exists. A dataset is a JSONL file named in a pack manifest. All 27 dataset files are tracked in git and inside pack digests, so none is hidden and none can become hidden. Treat them as public regression sets.
- src/mendari/evals/agreement.py compares two machine variants; it is not rater agreement. The review queue shows the model's answer and takes one resolution per decision, so it cannot be used for blind labelling.
- Sealed inputs with chained reads exist for encrypt mode in src/mendari/core/phi.py and audit.py; copy that pattern.
- Owner decision needed before starting: where hidden-set content, labels and keys live. They must be outside this repository, outside every pack directory and outside every pack digest. If tasks/todo.md does not record the decision, stop and ask.
- Requires LP-10. Needs no accounts work: the labeller and grading roles can be granted through the existing role mapping.

Requirements:
1. Tables for sets, cases, assignments and labels. Each case records its source (hand-written or de-identified real), its sampling stratum and weight, language, and author or template. No hidden case may come from a public generator or template bank.
2. Labelling: each case is labelled independently by two clinicians. A labeller's routes return the case input only: never another label, a model output, the author's intended label or case attributes. Disagreements go to a third adjudicator.
3. Audit chain: each label event chains a keyed hash of the case id and of the label values, plus the labeller and time. The clear values stay in the sealed store.
4. Agreement per question: raw percent agreement; Cohen's kappa per rater pair; Krippendorff's alpha across the pool, since pairs vary; a percentile bootstrap 95% interval (2,000 resamples of cases, fixed seed).
5. Lifecycle: draft (mutable), frozen (immutable, content hash over case id, canonical input and final labels, signed with a commitment published at freeze), active (frozen and in use for grading), retired (may be published). Only frozen or active sets can grade. A rotation rule: a maximum number of grading runs or a maximum age, whichever comes first.
6. Access: a machine-only grading role holds the only permission that reads active or frozen content. Every read is chained. Results from hidden sets have an aggregates-only shape: no per-case field, no missed ids.
7. A keyed-HMAC index of every hidden case's canonical input (and a normalised-text variant) for the upload check in LP-20.
8. Coverage report per set: cases per label and per critical label, distinct positives after duplicate removal, language mix, source mix, and a warning under 100 distinct positives for a critical label. Ship it also as a CLI over any JSONL file.
9. A CLI and a dashboard screen to sample, assign and track labelling progress.

Tests:
- Generated from the app's route list: for every route, every role and every key scope, no response, export or log line contains active or frozen set content, except through the grading role.
- A second labeller's request never returns the first labeller's answer or any model output.
- Kappa and alpha match hand-computed references on a fixture; the bootstrap interval matches a reference to the stated tolerance.
- Freezing fixes the content hash; any later change is refused.
- mendari audit verify passes after labelling, adjudication and freeze, on SQLite and Postgres.

Acceptance: all of the above in CI, using synthetic fixture sets only.

Do not:
- Do not put any hidden case, label or key in the repository, a pack directory or a test fixture that ships.
- Do not reuse the review queue's screens for labelling; they show model output.
- Do not chain label values in clear.
- Do not allow a person, or the admin role, to hold the grading permission.
```

```text
LP-12. Accounts, memberships and invitations on the existing tenancy (amends A6)

Goal: each partner team works in its own organization without the owner editing settings or redeploying, and nobody lands in an organization by default.

Context:
- Tenancy exists: the organization comes from an identity-provider claim (src/mendari/core/tenancy.py, src/mendari/server/auth.py, sessions.py). Decision data is scoped. There are no user, membership or invitation records. Roles come from MENDARI_OIDC_ROLE_MAP, read once at start-up.
- A sign-in with no organization claim is placed in "default", which is therefore a catch-all, not the owner's private organization.
- The audit chain is one global chain; audit_events has no organization column.
- Requires LP-08.

Requirements:
1. Two modes. "claims" is today's behaviour, kept for self-hosting. "memberships" is for the hosted site: users (issuer and subject, verified e-mail), organizations as records, memberships holding a set of roles. Reserve role names for pack author, labeller and grading. Additive schema only.
2. In memberships mode a sign-in without a membership gets no organization and a real "you don't have access" page with a way to request access. The id "default" is reserved for the owner's existing data. An idempotent bootstrap command creates the owner's membership.
3. Invitations: a single-use, expiring link bound to an e-mail address, shown to the inviting admin to send. Accepted only by a signed-in user with that verified e-mail. No e-mail is sent by Mendari. The deployment operator creates organizations until self-service (stage 5).
4. Every read, write, export and audit route is filtered by the caller's organization. Bind the organization into each chained event and give each organization a scoped reader and export for its administrative events.
5. An Access screen for API keys and Backend Services clients: create, scope, rotate (LP-09), revoke, history. Keep hashing, expiry and one-time display. Key events are chained.
6. Key presets, one per documented integration path, defined in one table that drives the API, the screen and the docs. Fix: a decide-scoped key can list packs and read CDS discovery (extend ADR-0004).
7. A daily cap per principal beside the per-organization quota, checked before any work, answering 429 with Retry-After.

Tests:
- Generated: every route is called as two organizations and no response, export or audit item contains the other's data. Deployment-wide routes are on a reviewed allow-list with the reason each holds no organization data.
- A sign-in with no membership, an empty claim or the literal value "default" gets no organization.
- Browser: the full invitation flow, including an expired link and a wrong e-mail.
- Each documented integration key works exactly as the docs say.
- mendari audit verify passes after membership, invitation and key events.

Acceptance: all of the above on SQLite and Postgres; claims mode behaves exactly as before.

Do not:
- Do not place anyone in an organization by fallback in memberships mode.
- Do not send e-mail or add a mail dependency.
- Do not migrate or rewrite the owner's existing rows; the bootstrap adds a membership only.
- Do not use real e-mail addresses in tests or fixtures.
```

```text
LP-13. Pre-public files and release gates (amends A1 steps 2 to 4)

Goal: the repository can be published without leaking internal material, and nothing can be released without an explicit, reviewed action.

Context:
- Already present: LICENSE (Apache-2.0), CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md (Contributor Covenant 2.1), SECURITY.md (GitHub private vulnerability reporting, no address), scripts/check_sdist.py, tests/test_packaging.py, tests/test_public_tree.py, environment: keys on the publishing jobs.
- Missing: NOTICE; any DCO text or check (CONTRIBUTING.md says "There is no separate agreement to sign"); a 90-day disclosure window; content checks on the wheel, the docs site and the image; SPDX headers (0 of 229 Python files). images.yml still starts on any v* tag.
- Owner decisions needed first: the copyright holder's name; publish the existing history or start the public repository from a fresh commit; whether tasks/ and notes/ go public. If tasks/todo.md does not record them, stop and ask.

Requirements:
1. NOTICE: project name, copyright line, and third-party attributions for everything vendored or bundled (Swagger UI, Lucide, fonts, HL7-derived material, terminology notices). Generate it from a small manifest checked by a test. Add it to the sdist allow-list, the wheel and the image.
2. One checker for excluded paths (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .claude/, tasks/, notes/, .github/) run against the sdist, the wheel, the built docs site and the image, in CI and in release.yml.
3. DCO: rewrite the licence section of CONTRIBUTING.md (inbound equals outbound Apache-2.0; every commit signed off under DCO 1.1; how to fix a missing sign-off; who can merge pack and engine pull requests). scripts/check_dco.py checks every non-merge commit in the pull request's own range, exempting named bots, effective from a named commit. A workflow job runs it.
4. SECURITY.md: add a 90-day coordinated-disclosure window; keep the private reporting form and the response commitment. Serve /.well-known/security.txt from web/.
5. Release: fold image publishing into release.yml behind the tested, version-checked build. No workflow publishes on a tag push alone: publishing needs a manual dispatch input and a protected environment, and a job-level guard refuses when the repository is private or the environment has no reviewer evidence. Remove the automatic v* trigger from images.yml.
6. SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0 on every source file under src/, tests/, scripts/ and hub/ (not vendored files), with a test.
7. Version in one place, a root CHANGELOG.md required by the release workflow.

Tests: check_dco.py against a scratch git repository with one signed and one unsigned commit; the excluded-path checker against fixture archives containing each excluded path; tests/test_workflow_safety.py extended to the new triggers and guards; the NOTICE manifest test; the SPDX test.

Acceptance: packages, docs and image build with no excluded path; no publish can start from a tag push; the DCO job fails an unsigned commit.

Do not:
- Do not rewrite history or delete branches.
- Do not publish, tag or dispatch a release.
- Do not publish an e-mail address the owner has not supplied.
- Do not add headers to vendored third-party files.
```

```text
LP-14. A redistributable reference pack and a tested licence inventory (new)

Goal: every reference pack can be redistributed under Apache-2.0 anywhere, and every dependency's licence is checked by a test.

Context:
- packs/prior-auth-preflight names 15 AMA CPT codes in its logic, mapping, manifest and data, generated by src/mendari/sandbox/synthetic_prior_auth.py. CPT is proprietary to the AMA. Whether it may ship is counsel's question; a fictional code system removes it.
- No licence check runs in CI. Some runtime dependencies may be copyleft; the audit flagged the Postgres driver for review.
- Pack artifacts are derived: follow the pack-artifact rule in the project instructions.

Requirements:
1. Replace CPT codes in the pack and its generator with a fictional local code system under a mendari.co URL, clearly named as fictional. Rebuild with ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh and commit data, model, benchmark and Decision Card together. Bump the pack version and changelog.
2. Manifest fields, validated: license as an SPDX expression; the code systems a pack uses. Refuse a pack that names a code system on a deny-list of proprietary systems unless a licence note is present.
3. A test that walks the locked runtime dependency closure with importlib.metadata against an allow-list of licences, and the same for hub/package-lock.json. Unknown or copyleft fails with the package named.
4. If a copyleft driver is a hard dependency, move it to an optional extra with a clear error when it is missing.
5. A test that no CPT, SNOMED CT or other deny-listed code-system URL appears in tracked packs, datasets or fixtures.

Tests: the two inventory tests; the deny-list test; mendari test on all five packs; the prior-authorization pack decides its examples with the same outcomes as before, code names aside.

Acceptance: no proprietary code system in the tree; the licence tests run in CI.

Do not:
- Do not change the pack's decision logic beyond the code values.
- Do not decide the legal question; record it for counsel in tasks/todo.md.
- Do not add a licence-scanning service or a network call to CI.
```

```text
LP-15. Deliver what the docs promise, and tighten the local loop (amends A2 steps 1 to 5)

Goal: a stranger can install Mendari and reach a first decision from the docs alone, and the create-improve-test loop cannot pass by accident.

Context:
- Version 0.1.0 is already set. The README installs from a clone and no longer promises pip install then pull. mendari new prints a guide URL. Image tags read v0.1.0.
- scripts/check_doc_snippets.py and scripts/run_quickstart.py run a curated set, not every snippet. There is no AI Guard example in the snippet set. No docs deploy workflow and no site_url exist.
- Found by running the CLI: behaviour tests accept unknown expect keys and empty expectations; a provider override for an unknown slot does nothing; validate does not read declared datasets; several subcommands show tracebacks; there is no --version or --debug.
- Requires LP-13 for the release path.

Requirements:
1. A manually triggered or rc-tag job that publishes a pre-release to TestPyPI through its own protected environment, then installs it in a clean runner and runs mendari test on a reference pack. Multi-arch images built in the release workflow.
2. A docs deploy workflow and site_url, after the owner names the host. Then point the scaffold and the README at it.
3. Every fenced bash or python block in README.md, docs/index.md and the /docs landing is either run in CI on a clean runner or carries an explicit, reviewed skip marker with a reason. A test fails on an unmarked, unrun block.
4. A CI-run AI Guard example (HTTP and Python) in the snippet set, the README, the API guide and the /docs landing.
5. Docs: the server install, the packs-folder setting, and the inbox rule as enforced by LP-01.
6. One top-level error handler for every subcommand: one line per problem naming the file, the field and the fix; tracebacks only with --debug. Add --version.
7. Behaviour tests reject unknown expect keys and empty expectations, naming the case. A provider override for an unknown slot is an error. validate checks every declared dataset: JSON per line, input against the schema, labels within the declared answers.
8. Open the database read-only for verify, export and monitor, and fail when the file does not exist.

Tests: the snippet-coverage test; for each subcommand, a broken input gives one line and exit code 1 without --debug and a traceback with it; the strict expect test; the unknown-slot test; a clean-runner job that reaches a REST, a FHIR and an AI Guard decision by running the documented commands.

Acceptance: the snippet job passes on a clean runner; no CLI error shows a traceback without --debug.

Do not:
- Do not publish to PyPI or deploy the docs; add the workflows only.
- Do not mark a snippet skipped to make the job pass without a reason a reviewer would accept.
- Do not change what a valid pack does.
```

#### LP-16. Pattern time limits and a decision budget

Amends A3. Full revised text.

```text
You are working in the Mendari repository on a new branch from main.
Before changing anything, read the project instructions file, docs/spec/decision-pack-0.2.md, docs/guides/authoring-a-pack.md and tasks/todo.md.
Non-negotiable rules:
- No mocks, stubs or placeholder calculations in product code. Every computation and statistic is real and deterministic (seeded where random).
- Every change ships with tests. The full suite, browser tests and all CI jobs stay green. Add new CI jobs where this prompt asks.
- Update the docs, the generated capability statement and the Use Cases page for anything that changes behaviour.
- Synthetic data only. Never touch production or the hosted deployment.
- Never weaken an existing safety rule, cap or check to make a test pass.
- If something in this prompt conflicts with the code or is unsafe, stop and write the conflict to tasks/todo.md instead of guessing.
Finish with: what changed, the test evidence (commands and counts), anything not done, and open questions.

Corrections to the header above, from the audit of commit e55ed48c (notes/vision-audit.md):
- There is no Use Cases page. "Update the docs" means docs/, README.md, the /docs landing (src/mendari/dashboard/docs.html and src/mendari/dashboard/snippets) and, for a new FHIR surface, CAPABILITIES in src/mendari/standards/capability.py.
- This prompt's id starts with LP-. tasks/todo.md uses A1-A11 and B1-B10 for a different plan; do not confuse them.
- The change must pass the "Definition of shipped" in notes/vision-audit.md, Phase 4.
- Local checks: uv run ruff check src tests && uv run ruff format --check src tests; uv run mypy src tests; uv run pytest (about 200 s) on SQLite and again with MENDARI_TEST_DATABASE_URL set to Mendari's own Postgres container; uv run pytest -m browser; uv run python scripts/check_doc_snippets.py; ./scripts/build-reference-pack.sh when a pack, a generator or policy code changes.

LP-16. No pack can tie up the server (amends A3)

Goal: no pack-authored pattern or provider can hold a worker, and a decision that runs out of budget goes to a person.

Context:
- Done in PR #127 and held by tests: a pack cannot name a secret, an environment variable or an endpoint; archives and YAML are bounded; a bad pack no longer stops the server.
- Not done: every pack pattern runs on Python re with no time limit (src/mendari/core/patterns.py, src/mendari/providers/rules.py, src/mendari/core/input_schema.py). A pack with (a+)+$ validates and stalls a worker. There is no time budget on a decision; CallBudget in src/mendari/core/decision.py counts calls, not time.
- RE2 is not a drop-in: four shipped patterns use lookaround, 53 use \b and 36 use \w, \s or \d (Unicode meaning differs), and the schema validator runs patterns on re regardless. A nested input schema (internal $refs under allOf, 28 levels) stalls a decision with no pattern at all. The unmodified inbox pack takes 563 ms of CPU on one crafted 10,000-character message. A CPU budget cannot be enforced in-thread on re.
- The pack still chooses the model: a pack's pinned model beats the deployment's LLM_MODEL, and omitted state_fields sends the whole input.
- Owner decision needed first: Option A or Option B below. If tasks/todo.md does not record it, stop and ask.
- This must merge before any outside author's pack is served anywhere, community packs included.

Requirements:
1. Outcome, with the owner's chosen mechanism written in an ADR: no pack-authored evaluation (patterns and schema validation) takes more than a fixed CPU bound on a 10,000-character input.
   - Option A: google-re2 for rules patterns and a custom schema pattern keyword; rewrite the four lookaround patterns; prove every reference decision is unchanged or rebuild the affected packs; pin the dialect in the spec.
   - Option B: keep re and run pack-authored work (schema check, local providers) for packs the deployment does not own in a pooled worker process with CPU-time and memory limits and a parent-side kill.
2. A blown budget is a ProviderError (budget_exhausted): policy sends the case to HUMAN_REVIEW, never AUTO.
3. A wall-clock deadline across the providers of one decision.
4. /api/health does no pack-dependent work in the request: it reports state computed at start-up or in the background.
5. load_pack is the single choke point: every variant and composite member is validated at load against its provider's closed config model (extra keys refused for rules, naive_bayes, criteria and dosing too), so load and validate agree. Publish the list as a generated JSON Schema. For packs the deployment does not own, the deployment's model wins over a pack pin, the deployment caps max_tokens, timeout and effort, and state_fields is required on remote variants. A provider egress allow-list in server configuration. Prefetch templates must be relative FHIR reads or searches. Pack wording templates accept bare {name} placeholders only.
6. Bound input-schema depth and reference expansion at load.
7. Threat model: a "Pack content" part of docs/security/threat-model.md (inputs, trust boundaries, what a pack can and cannot do), held by tests/test_threat_model.py.

Tests:
- (a+)+$ and a generated family of nested-quantifier patterns on 10,000 and 100,000 characters: rejected at validation, or finished within the bound measured as CPU time, or killed with budget_exhausted. Assert on CPU time or on linear scaling, not wall-clock alone.
- hypothesis (dev dependency): for any generated pack that loads, every outbound request's origin and Authorization equal what the test environment fixed.
- /api/health answers within its bound while a pathological pack is loaded and while a decision on it is running.
- The existing exploit regression test still passes.
- A pack that validate refuses is never listed as valid by load_pack (parity test).
- The nested-$ref schema is refused at load or killed within the budget.

Acceptance: all four in CI; the ADR and the threat-model part reviewed in the pull request.

Do not:
- Do not rely on a thread timeout or a signal to stop re.
- Do not change the answer of any reference pack decision without rebuilding and committing its artifacts.
- Do not weaken the checks added in PR #127.
```

```text
LP-17. Scoreboard and grading service (amends A9)

Goal: a reproducible, model-agnostic scoreboard and a scoped grade for every pack, built so it cannot be gamed by selection or by repeated submission.

Context:
- Requires LP-10 (statistics, ledgers) and LP-11 (frozen sets, grading role).
- Today there is no arm concept: 166 leaderboard rows, none combining Jev and an LLM. Evaluation code is in src/mendari/evals/ (harness.py, leaderboard.py, compare.py, gate.py). Ed25519 signing in src/mendari/hub/archive.py is for publishers' pack archives.
- Owner decision needed: which parts of this are built in a private repository (the grading runner, its provider budget, the grading key). The method, the statistics and the verifier are open.

Requirements:
1. Method document docs/grading/method-1.0.md, versioned, with an effective date and a changelog. Its version and hash go in every signed result. Everything below is written there first.
2. Arms by provider class, derived from the pack manifest by walking each variant tree: deterministic only; plus a specialised classifier; plus a general LLM; plus both. Each named model is an entry within its class. An arm a pack does not support shows "not applicable".
3. Selection: each frozen set is split by a hash of the case id, stratified by critical label, into a selection part and a ranking part. The configuration per arm is chosen on the first and reported on the second. Or the developer declares one configuration per arm before grading.
4. Per arm, from the ledger: per-critical-label recall; critical cases automated; false automation (wrong AUTO over all labelled cases); AUTO error rate with its n; automation rate; accuracy; macro-F1 with a stratified bootstrap interval; Brier with its reliability term; ECE as a description only; cost per 1,000 decisions from tokens and a dated price table; latency p50 and p95, unranked; subgroups where the set has them. Enriched sampling is weighted back for prevalence-dependent metrics.
5. Statistics: Clopper-Pearson for proportions, simultaneous across an arm's critical labels (level 1 - 0.05/L). Exact McNemar for paired proportions; paired bootstrap for Brier. Holm over a stated family: per pack and set, all arm pairs by all ranking criteria by all critical labels.
6. Grade: a set of scoped attestations per arm, each met, not met or insufficient evidence. For example: no critical case automated; each critical label's recall lower bound at or above the pre-registered floor; false-automation upper bound at or below its ceiling. No letter, no composite score.
7. Ranking, separate from the grade: arms failing a safety floor are listed unranked. The rest are ordered lexicographically on bands fixed before data is seen (worst critical-label lower bound, false-automation upper bound, accuracy, Brier), then cost. Arms with equal band tuples share a rank. Show rank intervals.
8. Evidence: publish only when each critical label has enough distinct positives for the bound the grade claims, and never fewer than 100; otherwise "insufficient evidence". Public template sets are shown separately, labelled "regression check, not a ranking", with no Rank column.
9. Scope statement on every result: pack digest, set versions, model versions as reported by the provider, method version, date, and "engineering evaluation, not clinical validation". A disclosure field for any commercial relationship with a model provider.
10. Reproducibility: statistics recomputed from the same ledger and seeds are byte-identical. Remote arms run 3 to 5 replicates and report the spread and the case flip rate.
11. Signing: results are signed over canonical JSON with a Mendari grading key distinct from any publisher key, stored append-only by content hash with a supersedes link. A public verifier command.
12. Freshness: re-run an arm when its pack digest, set or effective model changes, or a small canary set shows drift; stale after 45 days otherwise.
13. Developer flow: submit a version; receive banded results only, never per-case fields; a submission budget per organization and pack lineage per set rotation; a sealed confirmation split scored once before publication; a log of every run.
14. Early scoreboard (stage 3): unranked, every figure with its interval, the insufficient-evidence banner, the scope statement, on public sets only.

Tests:
- Every interval, test and adjustment against scipy and statsmodels references (dev dependencies).
- The band order is a total preorder on a generated set of arms.
- A re-run from the same ledgers and seeds is byte-identical.
- A pack with 20 emergency positives shows "insufficient evidence".
- A tampered result fails verification; a result signed by a publisher key is refused.
- No route returns a per-case field from a hidden set.

Acceptance: the method document, the scoreboard for all five packs on public sets, and the tests above in CI.

Do not:
- Do not select and rank on the same cases.
- Do not blend criteria into one number or publish a letter grade.
- Do not rank on ECE, or publish a bootstrap interval for it on a small set.
- Do not put hidden-set content, the grading key or a provider key in the repository.
- Do not call live providers from CI.
```

```text
LP-18. A registry that shows only what it has checked (amends A10 steps 1, 3 and 4)

Goal: nothing on a pack's page comes from the uploader unchecked, an unsafe pack can be recalled, and a pulled pack cannot automate until someone approves it.

Context:
- src/mendari/hub/registry.py stores and serves the uploaded DECISION_CARD.md, benchmark scalars and leaderboard as they are. A risk-2 pack with a card saying risk 1 is accepted and shown (reproduced). There is no yank or delete, no limiter, no security headers, no audit events.
- The pull cache is on the pack search path (src/mendari/core/registry.py), and policy caps AUTO only by the risk level the pack itself declares.
- The Hub site is in hub/ (Next.js). Requires LP-16 before any outside pack is served.

Requirements:
1. The registry renders the card itself from the digested files only (manifest, policy, schema, changelog). Give render_card in src/mendari/core/card.py explicit inputs so uploaded attestations are left out. Uploaded cards, benchmarks and leaderboards are ignored.
2. A declared risk level is shown as "declared by the publisher" until a reviewer confirms it. It is never "recomputed".
3. Any figure the registry computes from a publisher's own test set is labelled "publisher's own test set: regression check, not a ranking". Only LP-17 results appear as a scoreboard.
4. Two labelled groups on every pack page and in the API: checked by the registry (signature, key, digest, card rendered here, review status) and claimed by the publisher.
5. Run the existing pack gate (scripts/pack_gate.py) on every publish, in an isolated job.
6. Yank as a recall: a signed, chained record with a reason and severity. A yanked version is hidden from latest and search, still fetchable by exact version with the reason shown. mendari pull and a running server both honour it.
7. Origin at pull: record the registry URL, namespace, publisher type and key id beside the verified marker. A pulled pack is capped at HUMAN_REVIEW in policy until the organization approves its digest.
8. Rate limits (reuse Limiter from src/mendari/server/limits.py); CSP, HSTS and frame-ancestors on every registry route and every Hub page; chained publish, yank and delete events.
9. The Hub renders Decision Card tables correctly, sanitises any publisher text, and shows the review-status label from LP-04.

Tests:
- A pack uploaded with a forged card, benchmark and leaderboard shows only registry-rendered values. The risk-2 pack with a risk-1 card is the regression test.
- A yanked version is absent from latest and search, and a server that pulled it stops automating it.
- A pulled pack never produces AUTO before approval, at any risk level.
- Explicit assertions of the exact header values on every registry route and Hub page, plus a scanner baseline in the hub-site CI job.
- A script tag in publisher text is rendered inert.

Acceptance: all of the above in CI.

Do not:
- Do not display any uploaded number, even with a caveat.
- Do not delete archives on yank; hide them and keep the record.
- Do not let the origin cap be lifted by a field in the pack.
```

```text
LP-19. Registry accounts and deployment (amends A10 steps 2 and 5)

Goal: publishing is tied to a signed-in person and a claimed namespace, and the registry and Hub run with the platform's sign-in.

Context:

5. Client trust: a registry key shipped with the client counter-signs accepted versions; HTTPS is required except on localhost.

- Do not keep the shared admin token as a publishing path.
```

```text
LP-20. Pack store: create, improve and test on the platform (amends A7)

Goal: developers and informaticists create, improve and test packs inside their organization on the hosted platform, with the same statistics as the scoreboard.

Context:
- The server loads packs from the file system once at start-up into one process-wide dict. There is no pack store, author role, upload, dataset upload, PHI detector or promotion. The governance approval that exists is permission to automate, asked for only at risk levels 2 and 3; it is not promotion.
- Hard prerequisites, checked by a test at start, not by reading: LP-16 (pattern safety and budget) and LP-12 (memberships mode) are merged. Also requires LP-10 and LP-11.

Requirements:
1. One validation function in the library, called by the CLI, the registry and the store.
2. Dataset checks as pure functions in src/mendari/evals/dataset_checks.py, shipped first as mendari data check PACK [FILE] and added to the packs workflow: schema per case; labels a subset of the declared answers; per-label n with its interval and a "too few to estimate" mark instead of a hard minimum; duplicates defined as equal canonical-JSON input plus a normalised-text variant; overlap with the pack's own training data.
3. A deterministic PHI detector over uploads and archive contents: structural identifiers (phone, MRN-like numbers, SSN, e-mail, street address, dates of birth) and a name list. It rejects over a threshold and reports what kind was found and where, never the value. Every shipped synthetic dataset must pass.
4. A hidden-set check using the keyed index from LP-11. A match is quarantined silently and reported to the operator; the uploader is not told which case matched.
5. A versioned store per organization in the database, keyed by content digest. Drafts and published versions are immutable. Uploaded evals and cards are discarded and recomputed for the digest.
6. A pack-author role. Authors upload a signed archive or copy a built-in pack, then validate and test as a draft. Drafts run under LP-16 plus a per-organization evaluation and provider budget.
7. Evaluation as a stored job with a ledger. Draft against current on the same cases: Clopper-Pearson for accuracy, per-label recall, false automation and automation rate; bootstrap for macro-F1 and Brier; exact McNemar and a paired bootstrap for the difference; the cases that changed, by id.
8. Promotion: its own chained, digest-bound record that every risk level needs, signed off by someone other than the author, naming the evaluation reports it relied on.
9. Export reviewers' corrections into an improvement dataset with each label's provenance, kept in the organization. Offer them as proposed behaviour tests for the draft.
10. Dashboard screens for upload, results, comparison and promotion, tokens only.

Tests:
- Browser, end to end: copy a pack, edit, test, compare and promote, in a second organization.
- An upload containing a hidden-set case is quarantined; an upload with PHI-like content is rejected with a report that holds no value.
- A draft in organization A is invisible to organization B.
- The author cannot approve their own promotion.
- mendari audit verify passes after every store event.

Acceptance: all of the above on SQLite and Postgres.

Do not:
- Do not open any upload route if the prerequisite test fails.
- Do not reuse the automation approval as promotion.
- Do not tell an uploader which hidden case matched.
- Do not store an uploaded card or benchmark.
```

```text
LP-21. Operations for a public service (amends A13)

Goal: the hosted platform can take strangers without the owner watching it.

Context:
- The public /api/health answer is three static fields and does no database work, so it stays green through a database outage (reproduced). mendari monitor exists and posts to a webhook; the self-hosted templates schedule it; the hosted site has no schedule. There is no docs/operations/ directory, no usage view, and no backup procedure.
- Per-organization quotas cover decisions only. Evaluations count against the deployment-wide daily budget, so one organization's admin can exhaust it for everyone.
- The firewall rule exists as a file in log mode in deploy/vercel. Requires LP-06 and LP-12.

Requirements:
1. A deep readiness route (for example GET /api/health/ready): one cheap database round trip, packs loaded, cached a few seconds, rate limited, no secrets. Status checks use it, plus the sign-in redirect, a synthetic decide on a reference pack, the registry and the Hub.
2. A guarded cron route that runs the monitor for every organization and posts alerts. Before scheduling, make the monitor compare like with like, and add operational health from stored columns: provider error share, abstention share, latency.
3. Alert formats chosen by a setting, with an HMAC signature. A failed delivery is retried and recorded.
4. docs/operations/incident-runbook.md, in the docs nav: severity levels tied to concrete triggers, who is paged, how to set an automation hold (LP-06), how to revoke keys, clients and sessions, how to notify users, backup and restore of the database with a chain verification afterwards.
5. A usage ledger per day, organization, provider type and purpose: calls, tokens, and cost with "unknown" shown as unknown. A usage screen for admins. Evaluations count against the organization that runs them.
6. Firewall: when the owner confirms enforcement, update the rule file and its test, and record the log-only results in the runbook.
7. Verify the audit chain incrementally from a stored checkpoint, and publish a signed daily checkpoint.

Tests: with the database unreachable the readiness route fails and the status view shows the outage; the cron route produces an alert from a seeded degradation and refuses a request without its secret; a restore from backup verifies; usage totals match a seeded day.

Acceptance: all of the above in CI; the runbook reviewed in the pull request.

Do not:
- Do not put database work in the unauthenticated /api/health answer.
- Do not record a dollar cost where the price is unknown.
- Do not enable the firewall rule or change any hosted setting.
```

```text
LP-22. Site evaluation network (amends A11)

Goal: health systems evaluate packs on their own chart-reviewed data, inside their environment, and share signed counts only, in a way that resists forged and selective results.

Context:
- Requires LP-07 (the closed counts-only schema), LP-10 (statistics) and LP-19 (organizations in the registry).
- Today a site is a free-text name, nothing is signed, and an identical body can be replayed. Suppression (MIN_GROUP = 10 in src/mendari/evals/share.py) covers subgroup cells and calibration bins only. No pooling or interval code exists.

Requirements:
1. Sites and site keys: a verified site record owned by an organization, with Ed25519 public keys, rotation and revocation. Reuse the signing code in src/mendari/hub/archive.py.
2. The payload from LP-07 is signed over canonical JSON with a key id, carries a nonce, and is verified on receipt. A replay is refused. A digest that is not a published version's is refused.
3. Evaluation tickets: before a run the site requests a ticket bound to site, pack digest, configuration and a keyed dataset commitment (an HMAC under a secret the site keeps). A ticket with no result is visible as unreported. One live submission per site, digest, configuration and commitment.
4. Suppression, one function over the typed payload with a written rule per field: the threshold with complementary suppression and a minimum dataset size for cells that describe people; safety counts handled by a stated rule.
5. Site evaluation defaults to the deterministic-only arm. A remote arm needs explicit consent recorded in the payload.
6. Aggregation per pack digest and configuration: per-site estimates with Clopper-Pearson intervals first; a pooled fixed-effect figure from summed counts, labelled as such; a random-effects estimate beside it; the range; the number of sites; a homogeneity warning from an exact or seeded permutation test when any expected count is under 5, chi-square otherwise, alpha 0.05.
7. On the scoreboard and Hub, site evidence is shown apart from hidden-set results, with site count and total cases, as a forest plot. It never enters the hidden-set rank.
8. mendari eval --share-out for review before sending, and an offline submission path.

Tests: a forged signature, an unregistered key, a valid signature over a different body and a replay are each rejected; a payload with any case text fails on both sides; pooled and random-effects intervals match hand-computed references; the homogeneity test matches a scipy reference; a suppressed cell cannot be recovered from the totals in a fixture.

Acceptance: all of the above in CI with synthetic site data.

Do not:
- Do not send a plain dataset hash.
- Do not accept rates from a site; accept counts and compute.
- Do not merge site evidence into the hidden-set rank.
- Do not use a chi-square test on small expected counts.
```

```text
LP-23. EHR pilot readiness (amends A12)

Goal: a real EHR can call Mendari's CDS Hooks services with its own signed token, and Mendari reads real EHR data shapes or says what it could not read.

Context:
- With sign-in on, an EHR-signed JWT is refused on discovery and on the hook (bearer credentials are capped at 512 characters, so a real token fails as malformed). In open mode the Authorization header is ignored. The narrow ehr principal exists only for SMART launches. Code: src/mendari/standards/cds_hooks.py, src/mendari/server/auth.py, scopes.py, smart_routes.py, src/mendari/standards/fhir.py (FieldMapping), fhir_client.py.
- Mappings read inline notes, the first coding, the payer identifier and MedicationStatement only. A failed prefetch read fails the hook with 422.
- The EHR scorecard is not in the repository; commit it with its rubric as part of this work.
- Vendor-specific parts need the owner's vendor choice and sandbox credentials. Build everything else against a test key pair acting as the EHR; mark the rest as waiting on the owner.

Requirements:
1. CDS client registry: per EHR, the issuer bound to its keys (by value or one pinned jwks_uri on the allow-list), an organization and allowed fhirServer bases. Verify signature, iss, aud, exp, iat and a jti replay check. jku is accepted only if it equals the registered URI.
2. A cds principal with exactly the permissions to read discovery and invoke a hook. The same credential reads discovery.
3. CORS for /cds-services and its children only, for registered origins, without credentials.
4. With sign-in off, /cds-services answers only on a local host or under an explicit sandbox flag.
5. Cards: selectionBehavior with suggestions; a feedback endpoint recording accepted and overridden cards, chained; plain-language text using the display names from LP-04; the notice from LP-05; engine detail behind the audit link; a link into the EHR launch view; referral cards only when action is needed; the inbox pack given a card template or excluded from hooks.
6. Mappings: fetch linked attachments through the allow-listed client; a system-aware code selector; the payer by Coverage reference; MedicationRequest with a stated derivation of start and stop; a per-organization site code map, versioned and hashed into each decision.
7. A data-coverage record on every decision: per mapped field, what was looked for and found, as counts and codes. A failed fetch, or data present but unreadable, degrades to ABSTAIN with a reason; the hook still answers.
8. Refresh tokens or iframe mode, as the vendor requires (owner-dependent).
9. CI: the Inferno Da Vinci CRD kit as the CDS Hooks client over HTTP with signed tokens; the named SMART App Launch and Bulk Data suites that apply to Mendari's role; the HL7 validator offline as the required gate and with a terminology server as a scheduled job; a scope check against the vendor's published scopes (owner-dependent).
10. A vendor-shaped synthetic profile for the sandbox: linked notes, several codings, MedicationRequest.

Tests: a valid token is accepted; a wrong issuer, wrong audience, expired token, replayed jti and unregistered jku are each refused; every reference pack with a standard hook decides on the vendor-shaped synthetic patients without a failed hook; a fetch failure gives ABSTAIN with a reason and a recorded decision.

Acceptance: the CRD client job passes over HTTP with signed tokens; the coverage record appears on every decision; the scorecard is committed with the rows this work changes.

Do not:
- Do not accept an unverified JWT in any mode.
- Do not fetch a URL that is not on the allow-list.
- Do not put note text in the coverage record or the audit chain.
- Do not use real patient data or a vendor's production endpoint.
```
